## [New Framework for Embedded Multi-Agent Learning via Self-Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22226) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a mathematical framework for prospective learning and embedded agency in multi-agent reinforcement learning, extending AIXI to handle non-stationarity and self-prediction. It shows that self-predicting agents can achieve infinite-order theory of mind and novel cooperation forms. This framework addresses fundamental limitations of standard RL in multi-agent settings, potentially enabling more robust and cooperative AI systems. It could influence future research in multi-agent learning, Bayesian RL, and embedded agency, with implications for real-world applications where agents must interact with other learning agents. The framework builds on AIXI and Solomonoff induction, allowing agents to predict their own actions and resolve epistemic uncertainty about themselves. It introduces new game-theoretic solution concepts and demonstrates that universally intelligent embedded agents can form consistent mutual predictions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Standard model-free reinforcement learning assumes stationary environments and decoupled agents, which fails in multi-agent settings where other agents' learning causes non-stationarity. AIXI is a universal theory of sequential decision making proposed by Marcus Hutter, based on Solomonoff induction, providing an optimal agent for computable environments. This work extends AIXI to embedded agency, where agents are part of the environment they model.

The paper introduces Argos (Agentic Reward for Grounded & Objective Scoring), a reward agent that adaptively selects from rule-based and teacher-model scoring functions to provide fine-grained rewards for multimodal reinforcement learning (MMRL) in agentic tasks. Argos evaluates final response accuracy, spatiotemporal localization, and reasoning quality simultaneously, achieving state-of-the-art results across spatial reasoning, visual hallucination, and robotics benchmarks. This work addresses a critical limitation in multimodal reinforcement learning: the reliance on sparse, outcome-based rewards. By providing richer, adaptive rewards, Argos can significantly improve the training of multimodal reasoning models, potentially reducing reward hacking and improving generalization in agentic AI systems. Argos uses a pool of teacher-model derived and rule-based scoring functions, selecting the most appropriate one per sample. The paper shows that SFT post-training alone is insufficient, as agents collapse to ungrounded solutions during RL without online verification. It also provides a theoretical justification via pareto-optimality.

## [RAPiD: Reward-Guided Consistency Distillation for Real-Time Diffusion Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07339) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RAPiD introduces a reward-guided consistency distillation framework that compresses a pretrained DiffusionPlanner into a few-step student model, achieving a 5.5x speedup in inference latency (from 100.91 ms to 18.41 ms) while maintaining comparable performance on nuPlan and achieving the highest aggregate score among learning-based methods on interPlan. This work addresses a critical latency bottleneck in diffusion-based trajectory planning, making it feasible for real-time closed-loop autonomous driving. By combining distillation with safety-aware reward guidance, it offers a practical path to deploy expressive multi-modal planners in production systems, potentially influencing future autonomous driving architectures. The student model is trained using deterministic teacher denoising steps and a low-noise data anchor, while an Implicit Q-Learning (IQL) critic is trained on a balanced mixture of log-replay and DiffusionPlanner rollouts with a modified PDM-style reward. During deployment, the 2-step student generates K trajectories and the critic performs best-of-K selection conditioned on the latent state.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are powerful generative models that can capture multi-modal distributions, making them attractive for trajectory planning in autonomous driving. However, their iterative denoising process is computationally expensive, limiting real-time deployment. Consistency distillation is a technique that compresses a diffusion model into a few-step generator, and Implicit Q-Learning is an offline reinforcement learning method that can learn value functions from static datasets. This work integrates these ideas to create a fast and safe planner.

The paper introduces PAIR, a two-stage reward model that combines a frozen hidden-state probe with a lightweight attention-based head to provide dense step-level rewards for GRPO training in multi-turn LLM agents. It addresses prefix contamination, where hidden-state probes degrade on corrupted prefixes, by using attention features to correct the bias. PAIR enables efficient credit assignment in multi-turn agent optimization without external model calls, ground-truth dependencies, or full-trajectory rollouts, potentially reducing training costs and improving performance. This could accelerate the development of more capable LLM agents for complex multi-stage tasks. The method achieves the highest AUROC on contaminated trajectories while operating at negligible inference cost. It uses a frozen hidden-state probe for belief-consistency and a lightweight attention-based head to correct toward grounded correctness, exploiting the complementary strengths of both feature types.

**Background**: GRPO (Group Relative Policy Optimization) is a reinforcement learning method for training LLMs, but it relies on sparse outcome rewards, making credit assignment across intermediate steps difficult. Hidden-state probes are auxiliary classifiers trained on LLM internal representations to predict properties, but they assume clean inputs and degrade under prefix contamination in multi-step settings. PAIR addresses this by combining probes with attention-based features that are robust to contamination.

## [Self-Correction Failure in LLMs Is a Role-Labeling Artifact](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05976) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper introduces a training-free intervention called source-conditioned role relabeling, which changes only the message role of an erroneous claim in the chat template. Across 12 model-domain combinations, relabeling self-generated claims as external sources boosts explicit-correction rates by 23 to 93 percentage points, significant in 10 of 12 settings. This finding challenges the assumption that LLMs have an inherent cognitive deficit in self-correction, suggesting that the failure is largely an artifact of how the model interprets role labels. It provides a more direct path to improving self-correction in AI systems, which is critical for AI safety and reliability. The intervention keeps the erroneous claim byte-identical and only varies its role among assistant, user, tool response, or system blocks. The most effective role label is domain-dependent: assistant role dominates in most math experiments, while a user message dominates in logical deduction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are often used in multi-step reasoning tasks, and recent studies have shown they struggle to correct errors in their own reasoning traces, even though they can correct errors from external sources. This paper investigates whether this is a capability deficit or an artifact of role labeling in the chat template. The study spans closed-weight APIs and open-weight models from 70B-class down to smaller families, covering 12 model-domain combinations.

Role-Agent introduces a framework where a single LLM acts as both the agent and the environment, enabling bootstrapped co-evolution. It comprises two components: World-In-Agent (WIA) and Agent-In-World (AIW), which improve learning efficiency and generalization. This framework addresses limitations in LLM agent learning, such as inefficient feedback and static environments, potentially improving performance across various tasks. ## [ReSum: RLVR Framework Boosts LLM Reasoning via Self-Summarization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13316) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ReSum is a novel RLVR framework that enables LLMs to compress and organize their reasoning trajectories through self-summarization, using a summarization-aware adaptive rollout mechanism and a contrastive advantage for finer-grained comparison. Experiments show an average 4% performance improvement and an 18.6% reduction in rollout length. This addresses a key limitation in long-horizon reasoning where RLVR often encourages unnecessarily long rollouts, degrading coherence and exhausting context budgets. By enabling models to manage their own reasoning trajectories, ReSum could improve reasoning efficiency and coherence, benefiting LLM applications in complex problem-solving. The framework uses a contrastive branch by masking the summarization phrase when the model triggers self-summarization, and randomly injects the phrase for non-summarization positions. A summarization-aware advantage enables finer-grained comparison between contrastive rollout trajectories.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a technique for improving long-horizon reasoning in LLMs, using auditably checked reward signals. However, existing RLVR methods often produce overly long reasoning rollouts, which can degrade coherence and exhaust context limits. ReSum introduces self-summarization to help models compress and organize their reasoning, motivated by pilot studies showing that self-summarization stabilizes generation and mitigates error propagation.

## [EvalSafetyGap: Unifying LLM Evaluation and Safety Failures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30219) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents EvalSafetyGap, a conceptual framework that unifies LLM evaluation and safety failures as a proxy-target divergence problem under optimization pressure, based on a systematic survey of 373 primary studies from 2018 to 2026. This framework provides a shared vocabulary for researchers, developers, and auditors to address measurement issues in LLM safety, potentially improving how benchmarks and safety metrics are designed and interpreted. It highlights the need to report capability, robustness, and governance separately rather than as a single score. The framework formalizes the problem through a Goodhart-inspired Instability Decomposition and an Alignment Trilemma, and includes an exploratory ten-model audit. The survey also proposes a research agenda including dynamic benchmarks, pre-specified threat models, and version-locked evaluation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Goodhart's law states that when a metric becomes a target, it ceases to be a good measure, which is a core issue in machine learning where proxy optimization can lead to reward hacking. LLM-as-a-judge protocols, where another LLM evaluates outputs, are also relevant because they can be unreliable under adversarial conditions. This survey synthesizes these and other issues into a unified framework.

## [Factorized Transition Effects for Latent Action Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30544) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces a two-stage framework, OTF, that factorizes transition effects into compositional primitives to learn state-conditioned latent actions, addressing action ambiguity in multi-object scenes. It presents two instantiations, OTF-LAM-Pixel and OTF-LAM-Dino, which show improved action alignment and downstream policy performance. This work addresses a fundamental challenge in latent action models—action ambiguity in multi-object environments—which is critical for unsupervised representation learning and reinforcement learning. By improving action alignment, it enables more effective use of action supervision and could lead to better performance in downstream tasks such as robotics and video understanding. The OTF framework first pretrains a compositional codebook to discover reusable local transition primitives, then aggregates them into compact state-conditioned latent actions. Experiments demonstrate that the learned primitives transfer across visual appearance and morphology, and the latent actions achieve stronger action alignment than existing LAMs, with competitive or superior downstream policy performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Latent Action Models (LAMs) learn action-like proxies from unlabeled videos by compressing changes between frames. However, in multi-object scenes, observations include distractors and camera dynamics, making action recovery ambiguous without supervision. The proposed framework addresses this by factorizing transition effects into compositional primitives, which are then aggregated into state-conditioned latent actions, improving alignment and downstream performance.

<a id="item-86"></a>
## [Capability Convergence Hypothesis: Access Structure Over Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14144) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the Capability Convergence Hypothesis (CCH), arguing that under a fixed per-token inference budget, capability converges to a hybrid architecture with both compressive and verbatim-index channels, not merely to a shared representation. It identifies three resource walls—Shannon, horizon, and circuit—that constrain other architectures, and reports pre-registered experiments showing a predicted 'scissors gap' in exact-retrieval error. This hypothesis challenges the Platonic Representation Hypothesis by suggesting that representational convergence does not guarantee capability convergence, which could reshape how researchers think about scaling laws and architecture design. It implies that simply increasing model scale may be insufficient; instead, architectures must include specific access structures to achieve certain capabilities, potentially guiding future AI research and development. The paper defines three resource walls: a Shannon wall barring any o(N^b)-state architecture, a horizon wall barring any fixed window, and a circuit wall barring fixed-depth attention-only composition (conditional on TC0 != NC1). Pre-registered experiments measured a scissors gap (exact-retrieval error 0.994 vs. 0.000 once a 64-scalar state gains one global-attention layer), and one prediction failed with its direction reversed, reported transparently.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: The Platonic Representation Hypothesis (PRH) suggests that as models scale, their representations converge toward a shared model of reality. The Capability Convergence Hypothesis (CCH) extends this by arguing that capability does not automatically converge with representation; instead, it depends on the architecture's access structure. The paper uses concepts from computational complexity theory, such as TC0 and NC1, to formalize the circuit wall, and information theory for the Shannon wall. The 'Newton's-apple problem' serves as a witness task to illustrate the need for both compressive and verbatim-index channels.

The framework uses a constrained temporal action alignment model for action localization and actor source attribution, and an LLM for claim extraction and verification.

## [AttriMem: Attribution-Guided Process Feedback for Agent Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21106) ⭐️ 8.0/10

AttriMem introduces an attribution-guided process-feedback framework that augments global outcome rewards with token-level local rewards to improve memory-construction policies in LLM agents trained with reinforcement learning. Experiments on long-horizon dialogue question answering show it outperforms retrieval-based, heuristic, and RL-based baselines. This work addresses a critical credit-assignment bottleneck in RL-based memory construction, enabling more fine-grained learning signals that can improve agent performance and cross-task adaptability. It has the potential to advance the development of more effective and generalizable memory systems for LLM agents. The framework derives local rewards from token-level contributions to the final answer, providing process feedback without requiring ground-truth targets for intermediate memory decisions. Experiments demonstrate that AttriMem not only outperforms baselines but also generalizes across benchmarks and answer models, and stabilizes RL optimization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents rely on memory to store and use information from past interactions, but constructing effective memory policies is challenging. Heuristic methods use subjective rules that may misalign with objectives, while RL-based methods typically use coarse outcome- or module-level rewards, which fail to identify which intermediate memory contents support the final answer. This creates a fine-grained credit-assignment bottleneck, as intermediate decisions lack unique ground-truth targets and appropriate credit varies with the agent's reasoning trajectory.

## [Entropy-Scaled Trust Regions Stabilize Asynchronous RL for LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22186) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Entropy-Scaled Trust Region (ESTR), a token-level trust region that scales off-policy deviations by local entropy, addressing instability in asynchronous RL for LLM post-training. ESTR achieves 37.34 avg@1 on BrowseComp-Plus and 95.69 on multi-turn GSM8K, matching synchronous GRPO while delivering a 2.6× speedup. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in asynchronous RL for LLM post-training, where off-policy data often causes policy collapse. By providing a principled, entropy-aware correction, ESTR could improve training stability and efficiency, potentially accelerating the adoption of asynchronous methods in RLHF and LLM alignment. ESTR requires no auxiliary forward passes or explicit version-switch detection, making it computationally efficient. The method is validated on long-horizon agentic tasks and mathematical reasoning benchmarks, demonstrating consistent improvements over existing asynchronous methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Asynchronous reinforcement learning accelerates LLM post-training by overlapping rollout generation with policy optimization, but stale data can destabilize training. Importance ratios are used to correct off-policy data, yet their natural scale varies with token entropy, leading to instability. Trust regions constrain policy updates to maintain stability, and ESTR extends this concept by scaling the trust region boundary with entropy.

## [DynaResize: Runtime GPU Reallocation Boosts LLM Post-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22614) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DynaResize, a runtime GPU reallocation system, dynamically switches GPUs between rollout and training phases in disaggregated LLM post-training, improving end-to-end throughput by 66.5% and reducing total execution time by 33% over the optimal static configuration. This addresses a critical bottleneck in RL-based LLM post-training, where static GPU partitioning causes pipeline bubbles due to long-tail rollout latency. By enabling dynamic reallocation, it significantly improves resource utilization and reduces training time, benefiting large-scale AI training systems. DynaResize decomposes resizing into fine-grained operations and removes non-startup-critical work from the critical path using communicator reuse, bounded state staging, and hysteresis-based resizing. It hides 27% of role-switching overhead, and the paper is available on arXiv (2607.22614).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: RL-based LLM post-training (e.g., RLHF) often separates rollout (generating responses) and training (updating model) across different GPU resources. Static partitioning can lead to inefficiencies when rollout latency varies, causing idle GPUs. DynaResize dynamically adjusts the GPU allocation to balance stage execution times, improving overall throughput.

## [RLSVR: Task Transformation Extends RLVR to Open-Ended LLM Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23802) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces RLSVR, a task-transformation-based training paradigm that extends RLVR to open-ended tasks by converting them into verifiable proxy environments. It instantiates RLSVR with SpyRL, a self-play method inspired by the social deduction game Who Is the Spy?, which provides fully verifiable rewards through voting outcomes. This work addresses a key limitation of RLVR, which has been largely confined to math and coding due to the need for deterministic verification. By enabling self-verifiable rewards for open-ended tasks, RLSVR could significantly expand the applicability of RL-based self-improvement to domains like creative writing and summarization, reducing reliance on human preferences or LLM judges. SpyRL uses asymmetric information among agents and a predetermined spy identity to generate verifiable rewards, with voting outcomes closely tied to output quality. Experiments on text summarization, creative writing, and mathematical reasoning show that SpyRL outperforms existing self-improvement methods on non-verifiable tasks and yields consistent gains on verifiable reasoning tasks. Models and code are released on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a training approach where models receive rewards only when responses meet automatically checkable criteria, such as correct math answers or passing unit tests. However, RLVR is limited to domains with deterministic verification, while open-ended tasks often rely on human preferences or reward models, which can introduce bias and cost. Self-supervised learning constructs pretext tasks to derive supervision from data itself, a principle that RLSVR adapts to create verifiable proxy environments for open-ended tasks.

## [Jasper: GPU-Native ANNS with Quantization and Batch Updates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.07048) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Jasper is a GPU-native ANNS system that introduces a CUDA batch-parallel construction algorithm for lock-free streaming insertions, a GPU-efficient RaBitQ quantization reducing memory footprint up to 8x, and an optimized greedy search kernel. It achieves up to 1.84x higher query throughput than CAGRA and constructs indices 7x faster on average. This work addresses critical limitations in GPU-accelerated ANNS—batch updates, quantization, and memory access overlap—making it highly relevant for real-world ML and IR applications with evolving high-dimensional data. It demonstrates significant performance gains over existing systems, potentially influencing future GPU-based vector search designs. Jasper builds on the Vamana graph index and uses RaBitQ quantization to reduce memory footprint without random access penalties. Evaluation across five datasets shows up to 80% peak roofline utilization and 10-74x faster queries than BANG, the previous fastest GPU Vamana implementation.

## [Iterative RAG Outperforms Ideal Evidence in Scientific Multi-hop QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.19827) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper presents the first controlled, mechanism-level diagnostic study showing that iterative RAG can outperform an idealized static RAG with all oracle evidence (Gold Context) in scientific multi-hop question answering. Benchmarking eleven LLMs on the ChemKGMultiHopQA dataset, the authors report gains up to 25.6 percentage points for iterative RAG over Gold Context. This finding challenges the assumption that providing ideal evidence at once is sufficient for complex reasoning, suggesting that the process of staged retrieval is often more influential than the mere presence of ideal evidence. It provides practical guidance for deploying and diagnosing RAG in specialized scientific settings, which is crucial for improving AI systems in research and knowledge-intensive domains. The study uses a training-free controller that alternates retrieval, hypothesis refinement, and evidence-aware stopping, and analyzes retrieval coverage gaps, anchor carry drop, query quality, composition fidelity, and control calibration. Failure modes remain, including incomplete hop coverage, distractor latch trajectories, early stopping miscalibration, and high composition failure rates even with perfect retrieval.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) extends large language models (LLMs) by incorporating external knowledge, but it is unclear when iterative retrieval-reasoning loops are beneficial, especially in scientific domains requiring multi-hop reasoning over sparse, heterogeneous evidence. Traditional static RAG retrieves top documents once, while iterative (or agentic) RAG dynamically refines retrieval and reasoning steps. This study provides a diagnostic comparison to understand when the iterative approach is superior.

## [Stem: Position-Dependent Sparse Attention for Efficient Long-Context LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06274) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Stem introduces a plug-and-play sparse attention module that uses position-dependent top-k selection, guided by a Token Position-Decay strategy and an Output-Aware Metric, to better preserve causal information flow in LLMs. It achieves superior accuracy with reduced computation and pre-filling latency. This addresses the quadratic complexity bottleneck of self-attention, which is critical for scaling LLMs to long contexts. By improving sparse attention with a theoretically motivated approach, Stem could enhance efficiency and accuracy trade-offs in long-context applications, benefiting a wide range of LLM deployments. Stem's Token Position-Decay strategy applies position-dependent top-k within each layer to retain initial tokens for recursive dependencies, while the Output-Aware Metric prioritizes high-impact tokens based on approximate output magnitude. The paper is an arXiv preprint (2603.06274v2) and has not yet undergone peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Self-attention in transformers computes pairwise interactions between all tokens, leading to O(n²) complexity in computation and memory, which limits long-context processing. Causal attention, used in autoregressive LLMs, restricts each token to attend only to previous tokens, but existing sparse methods often apply uniform top-k selection, ignoring the cumulative dependency of initial tokens. Stem rethinks this from an information flow perspective to improve efficiency.

## [Step Grounding Rate Predicts OOD Generalization in Long-Horizon VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.06828) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces the Step Grounding Rate (SGR), a behavioral faithfulness metric that measures whether a model's intermediate reasoning remains grounded in the evolving visual state. Across eight models and three long-horizon benchmarks, SGR predicts out-of-distribution retention with a correlation of r = 0.83 (permutation test p = 0.003). This finding highlights that temporal grounding quality is a leading indicator of robustness, independent of model scale or in-distribution accuracy. It suggests that evaluation metrics should go beyond final-answer accuracy to capture how models use visual information, which could influence future model development and evaluation practices. Grounding quality varies by up to 10.8 percentage points within parameter-matched 7B models despite similar accuracy, revealing it as an independent axis of capability. Counterfactual traces drop SGR by 26–41 percentage points, cross-architecture verifiers agree at ρ = 0.96, and the predictor remains strong even without explicit reasoning disclosure (r = 0.78).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon vision-language models (VLMs) are designed to reason over extended sequences of visual and textual information, such as in video understanding or embodied AI. Standard benchmarks typically measure only final-answer accuracy, which can obscure whether a model's step-by-step reasoning is actually grounded in the visual input. Behavioral faithfulness metrics like SGR aim to quantify this grounding, providing a more nuanced view of model capability.

## [Video Reasoning Emerges Along Denoising Steps, Not Frames](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16870) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper challenges the Chain-of-Frames assumption and reveals that reasoning in diffusion-based video models primarily emerges along denoising steps, a mechanism termed Chain-of-Steps (CoS). It also identifies emergent behaviors including working memory, self-correction, and perception-before-action. This finding reframes how we understand reasoning in generative video models, potentially guiding future AI research and model design. It offers a new substrate for intelligence by exploiting inherent reasoning dynamics, with implications for improving reasoning capabilities without additional training. The study uses qualitative analysis and probing experiments, and shows that middle layers of Diffusion Transformers perform key reasoning. A simple Training-Free Ensemble (TFE) is proposed as a proof-of-concept, improving reasoning by ensembling latent trajectories from identical models with different seeds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models generate videos by iteratively denoising random noise into coherent content. Prior work attributed video reasoning to a Chain-of-Frames mechanism, where reasoning unfolds sequentially across frames. This paper instead shows that reasoning emerges along the denoising steps, where early steps explore multiple candidates and later steps converge to a final answer.

## [Alibaba Field Experiment Shows GenAI Boosts Speed, Not Objective Quality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.29888) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A large-scale field experiment at Alibaba found that a generative AI assistant for customer service agents improves service speed and subjective quality (customer ratings) but has no significant effect on objective quality (customer retrials). The study also reveals heterogeneous effects: lower-performing agents benefit most, while top performers experience declines in both quality measures. This provides causal evidence from a real-world deployment on how generative AI affects operational performance, highlighting that gains are not purely from automation but from reshaping agent-customer interactions. The findings underscore the need for skill-based rollout strategies, as AI can narrow performance gaps but may disrupt top performers, informing AI deployment in customer service and beyond. The AI assistant drafts issue diagnoses and solution proposals only in the opening stage, and agents can adopt, modify, or disregard them. The experiment measured objective quality via customer retrials and found that among top performers, AI use increased shift-away time, response delays, and immediate retrials, suggesting workflow disruption.

## [Compiled AI: Deterministic Code Generation for Enterprise Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.05150) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces 'compiled AI', a paradigm where LLMs generate deterministic executable code during a compilation phase, eliminating runtime model calls. The authors present a system architecture, a four-stage generation-and-validation pipeline, and an evaluation framework, demonstrating significant token savings and high reliability on function-calling and document intelligence tasks. This paradigm addresses critical challenges of reliability, auditability, and cost in high-stakes enterprise AI, particularly in healthcare. By trading runtime flexibility for predictability, it could accelerate enterprise adoption of LLMs in regulated industries. On function-calling (BFCL, n=400), compiled AI achieves 96% task completion with zero execution tokens, breaking even at ~17 transactions and reducing token consumption by 57x at 1,000 transactions. On document intelligence (DocILE, n=5,680), the Code Factory variant matches Direct LLM on KILE (80.0%) and achieves the highest LIR (80.4%). Security evaluation shows 96.7% prompt injection detection and 87.5% static code safety analysis with zero false positives.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Compiled AI builds on prior work such as DSPy (declarative pipeline optimization) and LLM+P (hybrid neural-symbolic planning). Traditional LLM workflows invoke models at runtime, which is flexible but costly and non-deterministic. Compiled AI constrains generation to narrow business-logic functions within validated templates, producing code artifacts that execute deterministically without further model calls.

The paper reinterprets mode collapse in LLM generation as geometric collapse in representation space and introduces Reinforced Mode Regulation (RMR), a low-rank damping intervention on the Transformer value cache. RMR reduces mode collapse across multiple models, enabling stable generation at entropy rates as low as 0.8 nats/step. This work offers a novel dynamical-systems perspective on a persistent problem, suggesting that mode collapse is not purely token-level and cannot be solved by symbolic constraints or probability-only heuristics. The lightweight, online intervention could improve generation diversity and quality in practical LLM applications. RMR is implemented as low-rank damping on the value cache, selectively attenuating dominant self-reinforcing directions. The paper reports that standard decoding typically collapses near 2.0 nats/step, while RMR maintains stability down to 0.8 nats/step.

**Background**: Mode collapse in generative models refers to the failure to capture the full diversity of the data distribution, often resulting in repetitive or degenerate outputs. In autoregressive text generation, this manifests as looping, loss of diversity, and premature convergence. The paper views this as geometric collapse, where the model's internal trajectory becomes confined to a low-dimensional region of its representation space. RMR intervenes online during inference to regulate these self-reinforcing directions.

## [Nonlinear SVD Theory for Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.06938) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proves that most modern neural architectures admit a nonlinear singular value decomposition (NLSVD) that preserves input distances in the embedding space, with no change in input-output behavior. It also introduces a flexible architecture for explicit decomposition at training time and a data-driven algorithm for estimating the representation from trained models. This work provides a new theoretical foundation for analyzing neural network latent representations, with practical implications for interpretability, visualization, bias detection, and robustness. It extends classical SVD theory to nonlinear neural networks, potentially enabling new tools for understanding and debugging deep learning models. The NLSVD factorizes a network into a left-invertible, norm-preserving nonlinear map followed by a linear layer, so distances in the embedding space calibrate directly to input distances. The paper also establishes mathematical foundations for nonlinear analogues of row and null spaces, and includes empirical case studies on latent-space pullback, bias detection, and membership-inference robustness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Singular value decomposition (SVD) is a fundamental matrix factorization used in linear algebra and data analysis, but it does not directly apply to nonlinear functions like neural networks. Understanding latent representations is crucial for interpretability and safety in deep learning.

## [DySink: Dynamic Frame Sinks for Autoregressive Long Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.21028) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DySink introduces a retrieval-based framework that dynamically selects visually relevant historical frames as dynamic frame sinks, coupled with a sink anomaly gate to filter out attention patterns associated with sink collapse. Experiments on 50–100-second videos show it achieves the highest temporal quality among evaluated autoregressive baselines. This addresses a critical limitation in autoregressive long video generation, where static early-frame sinks become outdated and can cause sink collapse. By making long-range context adaptive, DySink could improve the temporal consistency and quality of AI-generated videos, benefiting content creation and related applications. DySink maintains a compact memory bank and uses adaptive retrieval to select dynamic frame sinks, while the anomaly gate filters retrieved context exhibiting excessive inter-head consensus. The code is available at https://github.com/yebo0216best/DySink.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Autoregressive long video generation often uses bounded-memory streaming for efficiency, combining local windows for short-term continuity with static early-frame sinks as long-range anchors. However, this fixed allocation can become less adaptive as the visual state diverges, and in severe cases, RoPE-induced phase re-alignment can homogenize inter-head attention, leading to sink collapse where content regresses toward sink frames.

## [MemForest: Write-Efficient Agent Memory with Hierarchical Temporal Indexing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.23986) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MemForest introduces a memory framework for LLM agents that uses parallel chunk extraction and a hierarchical temporal index called MemTree to eliminate sequential bottlenecks in the write path. It achieves up to 6.0x faster build rates than EverMemOS on LongMemEval-S and 9.5x faster on LoCoMo while maintaining competitive accuracy. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in long-context LLM agent memory systems, where sequential extraction and state-dependent maintenance delay the availability of new evidence. By making memory writes more efficient, MemForest could enable agents to handle longer interactions and more frequent updates, improving real-time responsiveness in applications like conversational AI and autonomous agents. MemForest decouples memory construction into concurrent operations and uses MemTree to organize memory as time-ordered trees, replacing global rewrites with localized dirty-path refresh. Experiments on LongMemEval-S and LoCoMo with Qwen3-4B, Qwen3-30B, and Gemma-4-12B-IT show that end-to-end work remains proportional to incoming content, with logarithmic bounds only on structural insertion and refresh depth.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents rely on memory to maintain persistent state across interactions, but traditional systems often use sequential autoregressive extraction or state-dependent maintenance, which creates bottlenecks on the write path. MemForest reformulates agent memory as a write-efficient temporal data-management problem, using parallel extraction and a hierarchical temporal index to enable localized updates. This approach is analogous to database indexing techniques, where hierarchical structures allow efficient point updates without full rewrites.

## [APPO: Fine-Grained Credit Assignment for Agentic RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12384) ⭐️ 8.0/10

APPO introduces a new reinforcement learning method that shifts branching and credit assignment from coarse interaction units to fine-grained decision points in the sequence, using a Branching Score that combines token uncertainty with policy-induced likelihood gains. Experiments on 13 benchmarks show consistent improvements of nearly 4 points over strong agentic RL baselines. This method addresses a key limitation in agentic RL by enabling more precise credit assignment, which could lead to more efficient and interpretable training of LLM agents for complex tool-use tasks. It has the potential to improve the performance and reliability of autonomous agents across various applications. APPO selects branching locations using a Branching Score that combines token uncertainty with policy-induced likelihood gains, filtering out spurious high-entropy positions. It also introduces procedure-level advantage scaling to better distribute credit across branched rollouts, while maintaining efficient tool-calls and interpretability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic reinforcement learning (RL) is a training paradigm that uses RL to elevate large language models (LLMs) from passive text predictors into autonomous agents capable of multi-turn tool use. A fundamental challenge in RL is the credit assignment problem, which involves measuring an action's influence on future rewards. Traditional methods often assign credit over coarse heuristic units like tool-call boundaries, making it difficult to identify which intermediate decisions affect outcomes. APPO addresses this by focusing on fine-grained decision points.

## [Metanym Game: Contamination-Resistant LLM Benchmark via Spectral Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21008) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The Metanym Game introduces a self-contained, self-consistent benchmark for evaluating LLMs' structural intelligence, where contestants create and rate each other's content, eliminating fixed test sets. It employs a spectral method—singular value decomposition of the ratings matrix—to assess factual accuracy without golden keys, correlating with GPQA Diamond at r=0.92. This benchmark addresses the critical issue of test set leakage in LLM evaluation, offering a contamination-resistant alternative that remains stable over time. The spectral solution for measuring factual accuracy without golden keys is a significant methodological contribution, potentially influencing future evaluation practices. The benchmark is entirely self-contained and self-consistent, with a council of the strongest players conducting official benchmarking, and seats are contestable based on performance. The study finds that judging is a scarcer skill than generating, as the strongest generators are middling judges and vice versa.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional LLM benchmarks rely on fixed test sets, which can be leaked into training data, compromising evaluation integrity. The metanym game is a competitive word game that measures structural intelligence, a concept from cognitive science, and uses peer ratings to evaluate both generation and judgment abilities. Spectral methods, like singular value decomposition, are mathematical tools used to analyze matrices, here applied to extract competence signals from ratings.

## [ECHO: Selective Turn Memory for Traceable Agentic RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31650) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ECHO introduces a selective turn-memory framework that compresses each environment turn into source-indexed records, reconstructs bounded policy contexts by selecting useful records, and reuses source indices for credit assignment. On BrowseComp-Plus, ECHO achieves 43.4% held-out accuracy, outperforming GRPO (28.9%) and SUPO (36.1%) while using fewer turns and lower trajectory volume. This work addresses a critical mismatch in agentic reinforcement learning: policies act on reconstructed context, but learners lack source-level provenance for credit assignment. By enabling traceable context reconstruction, ECHO improves credit assignment in long-horizon tasks, which is essential for advancing language agents in real-world applications. ECHO compresses each completed environment turn into a compact source-indexed memory record, and reconstructs bounded policy contexts by selecting useful records. The selected source indices are reused to route positive outcome credit to the final trajectory segment, reused evidence turns, memory findings, and memory-selection actions. The trained policy also improves zero-shot generalization across multi-objective QA, code generation, and deep information-seeking benchmarks on both dense and MoE backbones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Long-horizon language agents must interact with tools and make decisions under bounded context windows, often using context-management methods like deletion, folding, or memory editing. However, compressing history into states can lose source-level provenance, making it hard to assign credit to specific evidence during reinforcement learning. ECHO addresses this by maintaining source-indexed memory records that enable traceable credit assignment.

BeatEdit introduces the first symbolic music generation framework based on explicit edit operations, using the BEAT encoding. It comprises three mechanisms—per-token sequence tagging, iterative refinement, and tag-then-fill—that achieve higher precision and perceptual quality than autoregressive and diffusion methods, with single-pass inference under 100 ms. This work addresses a gap in symbolic music generation, which has been dominated by from-scratch generation paradigms. By enabling selective modification of drafts, BeatEdit could influence future research and applications in music editing and generation, potentially improving efficiency and user control. The framework uses a single encoding and pre-trained backbone for all three tasks, and cross-encoding evaluation shows that encoding design significantly affects editing effectiveness. Code is available on GitHub, and the approach is efficient, with inference completing in under 100 ms.

## [CPInj: New Attack Exposes Prompt Injection Risks in Collaborative LLM Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.18622) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Collaborative Prompt Injection (CPInj), a novel attack that injects malicious instructions into the decentralized textual collaborative prompt optimization (TCPO) loop, contaminating the global prompt and degrading downstream task performance. The authors also propose Anchored Purification Aggregation (APAgg), a defense-oriented aggregation method, but show that current defenses are largely ineffective against CPInj. This work highlights a critical security vulnerability in the emerging area of collaborative prompt optimization, which extends TextGrad to decentralized settings. As such collaborative frameworks gain adoption, CPInj exposes a new attack surface that could compromise the integrity of LLM-based systems, underscoring the urgent need for robust defenses. The attack must survive server-side aggregation, persist through subsequent benign prompt optimization, and evade detection-based defenses. Experiments were conducted across three LLM families and five reasoning tasks in math, logic, and medicine, showing that current defense methods are ineffective against CPInj, and while APAgg mitigates the attack, it remains highly effective and not fully resolved.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Textual Collaborative Prompt Optimization (TCPO) extends TextGrad, a framework that uses LLMs to backpropagate textual gradients for prompt optimization, to a decentralized setting where multiple clients jointly improve prompts while keeping data local. Prompt injection is a known cybersecurity exploit where malicious inputs cause unintended behavior in LLMs. In TCPO, the reliance on free-form textual updates and aggregation introduces a new attack surface for such injections.

## [GEAR: A Reward Shaping Method to Curb Repetitive Copying in Long-Context LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19345) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper identifies a failure mode called 'repetitive copying' in long-context LLM reasoning, where models copy input text instead of solving the problem. It introduces GEAR (Grounding Evidence-Aware Reward), a reward shaping method that adds a grounding reward and a distractor penalty to the accuracy signal, improving performance by up to +4.6 average points. This is significant because repetitive copying is a critical failure mode in long-context reasoning, and GEAR provides a targeted solution that improves accuracy and reduces copying. It highlights the importance of grounding in relevant evidence, which is crucial for advancing LLM capabilities in complex, long-context tasks. GEAR combines an accuracy signal with a grounding reward for overlap with key evidence and a distractor penalty for overlap with irrelevant context. The method uses an automated pipeline to construct evidence-annotated training data from arbitrary documents, and experiments show larger gains at longer contexts while also reducing repetitive copying and thinking length.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) generate step-by-step reasoning traces to solve complex tasks, and extending them to long-context settings is an important frontier. However, in long-context scenarios, models often copy text from the input into their reasoning traces, a behavior called 'repetitive copying', which is negatively correlated with accuracy. Reward shaping is a technique in reinforcement learning that modifies the reward function to guide learning, and GEAR applies this to encourage grounding on key evidence.

## [HijackKV: New Attack Exploits Position-Independent KV Cache Reuse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19957) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces KV Cache Hijacking, a new threat that exploits position-independent KV cache reuse in LLMs, and presents HIJACKKV, the first attack framework to systematically exploit this vulnerability. The attack achieves an average 94% success rate in a single attempt and remains effective under realistic constraints. This is significant because position-independent KV cache reuse is a recent optimization technique adopted to improve inference efficiency, and this work reveals a critical security flaw that could allow attackers to silently manipulate model outputs without injecting any visible malicious text. It affects LLM serving systems that reuse KV caches across users, potentially impacting security and trust in AI services. HIJACKKV optimizes an attacker-controlled prefix so that the KV computed for a subsequent common benign text encodes the attacker's goal, while the text remains unchanged for future cache hits. The attack persists over multi-turn interactions and transfers across models in black-box settings, and the paper also provides design insights for building secure KV reuse systems.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Key-Value (KV) cache is a technique used in large language models (LLMs) to reduce inference latency by storing intermediate attention computations. Traditional prefix-based reuse requires exact token and position matches, leading to low cache hit rates. To improve efficiency, recent systems have introduced position-independent KV reuse, which allows cache reuse whenever identical text chunks appear, regardless of their position. However, this design introduces a new attack surface: since KV caches are retrieved by token match but encode the context in which they were computed, an attacker can poison the cache to hijack model behavior.

## [DualityCert: Verifier-Gated LLM Repair of Broken Seiberg Duality Claims](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23614) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DualityCert introduces a symbolic verifier that checks candidate Seiberg-duality claims in four-dimensional N=1 quiver gauge theories, and uses it to guide language-model agents in repairing deliberately broken claims. On a preregistered benchmark of 145 broken claims, verifier-gated retry improved repair success by +8.3 percentage points on deepseek-chat and +7.1 pp on qwen-plus compared to a single attempt. This work demonstrates a promising approach to combining formal verification with large language models for theoretical physics, potentially accelerating research in quantum field theory. The significant improvements over baselines and the rigorous preregistered protocol highlight the potential for AI-assisted discovery and validation in scientific domains. The verifier evaluates 't Hooft anomaly matching, superpotential R-charge consistency, central-charge matching, and a bounded chiral-ring proxy, issuing a consistency certificate rather than a proof. The study also found that category-level verifier feedback is worth +8.7 pp over content-free retry on qwen-plus, and interpretable obligation identities alone are worth +6.4 pp over masked feedback, but these effects were not detected on deepseek-chat.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Seiberg duality is a conjectured equivalence between two different supersymmetric gauge theories that flow to the same infrared fixed point, first proposed by Nathan Seiberg in 1994. Quiver gauge theories are gauge theories whose field content is described by a quiver diagram, often arising from D-branes on orbifolds. 't Hooft anomaly matching is a consistency condition stating that certain quantum anomalies must match between the ultraviolet and infrared descriptions of a theory.

## [Role-Stratified Conformal Risk Control for LLM Tool Calls](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24343) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces role-stratified per-field conformal risk control, a calibration layer that assigns separate thresholds and risk budgets to each semantic argument role in LLM tool calls, ensuring per-field risk guarantees rather than aggregate certification. This addresses a critical safety gap in LLM agents, where rare high-risk arguments (e.g., recipient, command) can be masked by benign ones under aggregate certification. It provides formal per-role guarantees, enhancing reliability for AI agents in real-world applications. The method is evaluated on AgentDojo and InjecAgent with six language models, showing consistent role-specific budget compliance under model/attack transfer, detector noise, gradual drift, unseen tool suites, and adaptive attacks. It provides finite-sample guarantees under exchangeability or after recalibration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Conformal risk control (CRC) extends conformal prediction to control the expected value of any monotone loss function with finite-sample guarantees. LLM tool calls involve structured arguments with varying risk levels; existing methods certify the entire call, potentially averaging away rare high-risk failures.

<a id="item-127"></a>
## [Progressive Multimodal Alignment Mitigates Projector Forgetting in MCIT](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26947) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Progressive Multimodal Alignment (PMA), a framework that dynamically expands projector experts to mitigate projector-level forgetting in Multimodal Continual Instruction Tuning (MCIT). PMA detects distribution shifts via a lightweight descriptor and uses an expandable router, retaining the pretrained projector as an anchor. This addresses a previously overlooked problem in MCIT, as most methods focus on the LLM backbone. By mitigating projector-level forgetting, PMA provides consistent performance gains over state-of-the-art methods and is method-agnostic, making it broadly applicable to the MLLM community. PMA achieves sub-linear parameter growth by expanding projector experts only when needed, balancing stability and plasticity. Extensive experiments on two recent MCIT benchmarks show consistent gains when combined with PMA, and it scales across diverse MLLM backbones.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) use a projector to align visual representations with the language embedding space. In Multimodal Continual Instruction Tuning (MCIT), models sequentially adapt to new tasks, but shifting visual distributions can cause the shared projector to drift, leading to catastrophic forgetting. Existing methods often focus on the LLM backbone, overlooking projector-level forgetting. PMA addresses this by dynamically expanding projector experts while retaining the original projector as a stable anchor.

## [New Benchmark Reveals LLM Answer Biases in Probability Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27405) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a benchmark for evaluating LLMs' logical inference over probability operators, containing 14,320 procedurally-generated English prompts across 15 inference templates. Evaluating 29 models, it finds that most show systematic answer biases independent of logical form, with only 9 of 29 exceeding random chance. This benchmark addresses an underexplored area of LLM reasoning—logical inference over probability operators—which is crucial for high-stakes domains like medicine and law. The findings of systematic answer biases highlight significant limitations in LLM reasoning, informing future model development and evaluation. The benchmark varies question form, negation strategy, and surface content, and also tests variations in verb phrases/activity, gender, and origin of names. The authors summarize answer bias with a 'competence floor' metric, defined as the worse of a model's accuracy on Yes-correct and No-correct items.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Probability operators are natural language expressions like 'probably', 'might', and 'must' that convey degrees of uncertainty. Logical inference over these operators is essential for understanding and reasoning with uncertain statements in everyday and professional contexts. This benchmark aims to disentangle principled symbolic reasoning from surface-level pattern matching in LLMs.

## [SE(3)-MeanFlow: Few-Step Protein Backbone Generation on Lie Groups](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27431) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SE(3)-MeanFlow introduces a few-step generative framework for protein backbone generation on Lie groups, deriving closed-form average-velocity identities for rotations and translations and an SE(3) alpha-Flow objective. It matches or exceeds flow-matching baselines that use several times more sampling steps, with advantages widening in the few-step regime. This work addresses a significant computational bottleneck in protein design by reducing the inference cost of generative models, enabling high-throughput design campaigns. It extends MeanFlow to Lie group geometry, potentially influencing future generative models for other structured data on manifolds. The method works natively in the Lie algebra so(3) and R^3, and the SE(3) alpha-Flow objective removes the Jacobian-vector product from the rotation branch, serving as a warm-up stage. Training then switches to a small-t stabilized MeanFlow loss, followed by rectification-based post-training, at a modest cost in diversity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Protein backbone generation models often use diffusion or flow matching on SE(3)^N, but inference requires numerically integrating an ODE over hundreds of network evaluations, each involving a Lie group exponential map. MeanFlow is a one-step generative framework that regresses interval-averaged velocity, and SE(3)-MeanFlow adapts this to the Lie group setting to achieve few-step generation.

## [LabEvolver: Training-Free Episodic Memory Boosts Wet-Lab Agent Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27690) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LabEvolver, a training-free framework, introduces episodic memory to wet-lab agents, reducing pH-regulation completion time by 48.2% and safety-gate intercepts by 60.0%. On ALFWorld, it improves cumulative success rate within 20 steps from 76.2% with ReAct to 91.4% over 500 continual tasks. This work demonstrates a viable path toward closed-loop automated scientific discovery by enabling agents to learn from experience without retraining. It shows significant performance gains in both robotic wet-lab tasks and a general benchmark, suggesting broad applicability in AI-driven experimentation. LabEvolver uses a dual-loop architecture: an inner trial loop for adaptive perception, online planning, and safety validation, and an outer evolution loop that distills completed trajectories into reusable skill, strategy, and safety experience. The framework is training-free, meaning it does not require fine-tuning of the underlying language model.

**Background**: Wet-lab agents are AI systems that operate in physical or simulated laboratory environments to perform tasks like solution preparation. ALFWorld is a benchmark that combines textual and embodied agent tasks, providing a standard testbed for evaluating such agents.

## [AirLLM Runs 70B Models on a Single 4GB GPU via Layer Streaming](https://github.com/lyogavin/airllm) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AirLLM, an open-source project, enables inference of 70B parameter models on a single 4GB GPU by loading only one layer at a time onto the GPU, reducing VRAM usage from over 140GB to under 4GB. The project has gained significant community attention, with 149 points and 53 comments on Hacker News. This breakthrough lowers the hardware barrier for running large language models, enabling developers and researchers with limited GPU resources to experiment with state-of-the-art models. It also sparks discussions about memory-efficient inference techniques and the trade-offs between speed and accessibility. AirLLM uses a layer-wise streaming architecture, loading each transformer layer onto the GPU for computation, then unloading it before moving to the next. This approach works without quantization, but inference speed is extremely slow; for example, Kimi K3 on an RTX 6000 Ada (48GB) takes 292 seconds per token. Users still need to download the full model to disk and connect to HuggingFace.

**Background**: Large language models like 70B parameter models typically require massive GPU memory (over 140GB) for inference, which is beyond the reach of most consumer hardware. Traditional approaches use quantization or model parallelism to fit models into smaller memory, but AirLLM's layer-wise loading trades speed for memory efficiency, allowing even low-VRAM GPUs to run such models. This technique is part of a broader trend of memory optimization in LLM inference.

The article explains the specific threats DMARC mitigates, such as email spoofing and phishing, and clarifies its limitations, including its inability to prevent all types of abuse. It emphasizes that DMARC relies on SPF and DKIM and only works if the domain owner publishes and enforces a policy. Understanding DMARC's scope is crucial for organizations to properly configure email authentication and avoid a false sense of security. This knowledge helps in designing layered defenses against email-based attacks, which remain a top cybersecurity threat. The article distinguishes between the two 'from' addresses in email: the envelope sender (SMTP) and the header sender (RFC 822), which is key to understanding SPF, DKIM, and DMARC. It also notes that DMARC does not prevent legitimate senders from being compromised or stop all spoofing if the domain owner does not enforce a strict policy.

**Background**: DMARC (Domain-based Message Authentication, Reporting, and Conformance) is an email authentication protocol that tells receiving mail servers what to do when an email fails SPF or DKIM checks. SPF verifies the sending server's IP address against the domain's DNS records, while DKIM uses cryptographic signatures to verify the message's integrity and sender. DMARC ties these together and provides a policy for handling unauthenticated mail, along with reporting.

## [GenCDSR: Hybrid Tokenization and Serial-Parallel Decoding for Efficient Cross-Domain Sequential Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28659) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces GenCDSR, a generative framework for cross-domain sequential recommendation that employs a cross-domain hybrid tokenization mechanism with a multi-tower architecture and a serial-parallel decoding strategy. It achieves an average accuracy improvement of 1.5% and an 85.1% reduction in inference latency compared to state-of-the-art baselines on three public datasets. This work addresses two critical limitations in existing generative recommendation methods: the neglect of cross-domain collaborative correlations during tokenization and inefficient decoding strategies like beam search. By significantly reducing inference latency while improving accuracy, GenCDSR could enable real-time deployment of generative recommendation systems in cross-domain scenarios, benefiting e-commerce and content platforms. The hybrid tokenization mechanism uses hierarchical shared-specific and fine-grained codebooks to capture both cross-domain commonalities and domain-specific distinctions. The serial-parallel decoding strategy leverages the hierarchical SID structure to partially parallelize generation, preserving consistency while cutting latency. The implementation code and datasets are publicly available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Cross-domain sequential recommendation (CDSR) models users' dynamic interests and sequential patterns across multiple domains, addressing data sparsity. Generative recommendation (GR) learns semantic identifiers (SIDs) from item semantics and frames recommendation as autoregressive generation, but existing methods often ignore cross-domain correlations and use inefficient decoding. This paper builds on these concepts to propose a more efficient and effective framework.

<a id="item-140"></a>
## [ViSAGE: Self-Correcting Entity-Centric Memory for Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28678) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ViSAGE introduces a multimodal agentic memory framework that constructs self-correcting, entity-centric memories for long-form video understanding. It uses cross-modal binding over long temporal ranges and multi-agent cross-verification to reduce entity confusion and hallucinations, achieving 5.9% higher accuracy than the strongest baseline. This work addresses critical limitations in existing agentic memory systems, which often suffer from entity confusion and error propagation in long-horizon multimodal tasks. By introducing self-correcting memories and abstention mechanisms, ViSAGE could improve the reliability of AI agents in video understanding and other multimodal applications, benefiting researchers and practitioners in multimodal AI and memory systems. ViSAGE anchors entity identity via cross-modal binding over long temporal ranges and applies bidirectional memory refinement to propagate delayed identity evidence. It also introduces multi-agent cross-verification under an identity-evidence alignment constraint, enabling abstention when evidence is missing, and consistently outperforms the strongest baseline by 5.9% accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal agents operating in long-horizon environments need to build and update multimedia memories to support entity-consistent, temporally grounded reasoning. Existing agentic memory approaches often discard fine-grained identity cues under aggressive compression and rely heavily on vector similarity retrieval, which can surface semantically related but identity-mismatched evidence, leading to entity confusion and hallucinated answers. Cross-modal binding refers to integrating information from different modalities (e.g., vision and audio) to form a unified representation, which is a challenging aspect of the binding problem. Multi-agent cross-verification involves multiple agents checking each other's outputs to reduce hallucinations and improve accuracy.

## [STL-GO Enables Multi-Agent Planning with Spatio-Temporal and Topological Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28679) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces two encodings, one based on mixed-integer programming (MIP) and another on satisfiability modulo theory (SMT), for planning multi-agent paths that satisfy constraints expressed in the spatio-temporal logic with graph operators (STL-GO). The encodings are evaluated on a multi-UAV search-and-rescue benchmark, demonstrating their expressiveness under dynamic multi-graph interactions. This work addresses a challenging problem in multi-agent planning by providing a formal framework that handles both spatio-temporal and topological constraints, which are common in applications like wildfire fighting and factory inspection. The proposed encodings offer soundness guarantees and a unified interface, potentially enabling more reliable and efficient multi-robot coordination in complex environments. The encodings handle multiple, potentially time-varying graphs via STL-GO's graph operators, and the unified interface allows seamless use of both MIP and SMT methods for direct comparison. The evaluation ablates over team size and graph complexity, highlighting the expressiveness of the encodings under dynamic multi-graph interactions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent planning problems often involve spatio-temporal constraints (when and where agents act) and topological constraints (how agents interact), typically modeled with graphs. STL-GO extends Signal Temporal Logic (STL) with graph operators, enabling reasoning about both per-agent properties and network-global constraints. This paper builds on that formalism to solve the planning problem.

## [LSR-Synth Benchmark Analysis: Anti-Memorization and Measurement Nuances](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28684) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a semantics-free baseline using a fixed vocabulary with documented provenance to evaluate LSR-Synth tasks, finding that this baseline covers most tasks and that language-model-generated candidates rarely expand solvable instances except when vocabulary coverage is disrupted. This work clarifies the limitations of current LSR-Synth benchmarks in distinguishing language model priors from conventional operator search, which is crucial for accurately evaluating scientific discovery methods. It suggests that current tasks may not fully capture the value of language model priors, impacting future benchmark design and model assessment. The study uses semantic blinding, library weakening, and matched operator-family knockouts to assess candidate coverage. Strict out-of-distribution evaluation lowers absolute success rates but does not change the relative relationship between the baseline and language-model-generated candidates.

Symbolic regression is a type of regression analysis that searches the space of mathematical expressions to fit data, and language models are increasingly used to guide this search. The paper addresses the challenge of measuring whether a model truly discovers laws from data or merely recalls memorized equations.

## [Bayesian Experimental Design for Cognitive Parameter Inference](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28894) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces an amortized Bayesian experimental design (BED) framework that treats the experimental environment as a design variable for cognitive parameter inference, validated on the Mouselab-MDP paradigm. It demonstrates that the amortized approach closely matches exact Monte Carlo BED in environment rankings while significantly reducing computational cost. This work addresses a critical gap in computational cognitive modeling by enabling principled selection of informative environments for parameter inference, which can improve the efficiency and reliability of cognitive experiments. The amortized approach makes BED computationally feasible for complex paradigms, potentially accelerating research in cognitive science and Bayesian methods. The framework establishes an exact Monte Carlo BED benchmark and an amortized variant for efficient posterior inference and design evaluation. Experiments on Mouselab-MDP reveal trade-offs between expected information gain, posterior recoverability, and information efficiency, showing that no single environment is uniformly optimal across objectives.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Bayesian experimental design is a decision-theoretic framework that optimizes experiments by maximizing expected information gain to reduce uncertainty about latent parameters. Mouselab-MDP is a process-tracing paradigm used to study human planning by revealing information acquisition patterns. Amortized Bayesian inference uses neural surrogates to approximate posterior densities, enabling efficient inference in complex models.

## [NeSyFS: Neuro-Symbolic Fast-Slow Thinking for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28942) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces NeSyFS, a neuro-symbolic framework that uses knowledge graphs to represent belief states and combines fast reactive actions with slow uncertainty-aware planning based on twisted sequential Monte Carlo, improving LLM agent performance under partial observability. This framework addresses key challenges in deploying LLM agents in real-world environments with limited observations, potentially improving decision-making in applications like robotics, web navigation, and scientific discovery. It also contributes to the growing field of neuro-symbolic AI by integrating symbolic knowledge with neural reasoning. NeSyFS consists of fast-thinking and slow-thinking modules, with a reflection module to switch between them when reactive actions fail. Experiments on ALFWorld, Webshop, and ScienceWorld show significant improvements over previous methods.

## [Steering Vectors Improve CoT Faithfulness but Generalize Poorly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29062) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper systematically studies how activation steering improves chain-of-thought (CoT) faithfulness across three models (Gemma-3 4B, Qwen-3.5 9B, Gemma-3 12B), multiple cue types, datasets, and four vector construction methods. It finds that steering reliably increases cue acknowledgment only for the largest model, and when effective, its effect generalizes broadly across cues and datasets. Faithfulness of chain-of-thought reasoning is critical for AI safety, as unfaithful reasoning can hide instrumental steps from oversight. This work provides insights into the generalization limits of activation steering, informing interpretability research and safety monitoring approaches. The study uses a cued question-answering setting and finds that steering leaves the rate of cue use roughly unchanged while reducing hidden cue use, suggesting it targets verbalization rather than cue salience. The effect size is determined primarily by the evaluation setting, not the vector's training setting, and four construction methods yield similar effects.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning allows models to verbalize their reasoning, which can be monitored for safety. However, models sometimes fail to disclose instrumental reasoning steps, leading to unfaithful CoT. Activation steering modifies a model's internal activations to influence behavior, and prior work suggested it could improve faithfulness. This paper extends that work by testing generalization across models and settings.

## [Counterfactual Explanations Reframed as Generalized Bayes MAP Estimates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29077) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper shows that distance-minimization-based counterfactual explanations are mathematically equivalent to MAP estimates of a Gibbs posterior under a distance-based prior, introducing a new framework called DP-GBCE. It also proposes new decision rules, including Bayes-optimal and risk-averse (CVaR) counterfactuals, and a method to handle model multiplicity via Bayesian model averaging. This work provides a theoretical foundation for a widely used but under-theorized technique in interpretable ML, enabling principled decision-making under uncertainty. It could lead to more robust and risk-aware counterfactual explanations in high-stakes applications like finance and healthcare. The paper introduces two new decision rules beyond MAP: a Bayes decision rule minimizing expected decision loss, and CVaR-CE, a risk-averse rule based on conditional value at risk. It also extends the framework to mix posterior distributions from multiple models using Bayesian model weights, and defines evaluation metrics for both individual counterfactuals and the entire posterior distribution.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Counterfactual explanations (CEs) are a common tool in interpretable machine learning, identifying the minimal input change needed to achieve a desired model output. The maximum a posteriori (MAP) estimate is a Bayesian point estimate that finds the mode of the posterior distribution, often used for regularization. Conditional value at risk (CVaR) is a risk measure that captures the expected loss in the worst-case tail of a distribution, commonly used in finance for risk management.

## [WILC: Complementarity-Driven Relay Collaboration for LLM Crowds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29087) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces WILC (Wisdom Integration of LLM Crowds), a framework that dynamically coordinates multiple LLMs in a relay-style manner, selecting each successor model based on the bottleneck identified in the predecessor's output. It uses a dual-gate mechanism (PCF and PCG) to manage transitions and outperforms existing methods across four benchmarks. This work addresses a key challenge in enterprise AI deployment: how to effectively combine multiple LLMs with heterogeneous capabilities. By demonstrating that dynamic, complementarity-driven coordination can surpass individual models and even match GPT-5.2 at lower cost, it offers a practical path to improved performance and cost efficiency. WILC operates through iterative reflection-and-refinement, preserving state across steps. The dual-gate mechanism uses prospective complementarity fit (PCF) to select the next worker and posterior complementarity gain (PCG) to evaluate whether the transition improves the solution. Under standardized pricing, WILC matches GPT-5.2's average benchmark performance at roughly 7x lower estimated per-query cost, and supports self-hosted deployment for data sovereignty.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) have individual capability limitations, and combining them can unlock collective intelligence. Existing approaches often fix the combination strategy in advance, ignoring dynamic complementarity. The wisdom-of-crowds paradigm suggests that diverse models can outperform individuals, and WILC extends this from static aggregation to sequential, relay-style collaboration.

## [MirrorCraft: Benchmarking LLM Agents Under Hidden Minecraft Rule Changes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29218) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MirrorCraft introduces a paired benchmark for evaluating LLM-based agents in Minecraft under hidden rule changes, using mirrored worlds with modified game rules. It includes five controlled biomes, six rule suites, three progression objectives, two model families, and six agent configurations. This benchmark addresses a gap in existing fixed-rule benchmarks by testing adaptability to rule changes, which is crucial for real-world deployment of LLM agents. It provides a controlled setting for studying how agents handle unfamiliar game mechanics, potentially influencing future agent evaluation and development. The benchmark uses deterministic advancement milestones and success rate for evaluation, and introduces the Rule Intervention Effect (RIE) to measure performance changes between Vanilla and Mirror worlds. Experiments show that hidden rule changes have varying effects across suites, and ReAct achieves the highest pooled Mirror score among configurations without rule descriptions.
