## [MAGA: Structured Action Distillation for Multi-Platform GUI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29320) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MAGA, a novel structured action distillation method that consolidates domain-specific GUI agents into a single cross-environment policy by reallocating training signals based on action correctness. It suppresses unnecessary or invalid distillation signals and focuses learning on erroneous actions, achieving the highest mean success rate across two model scales. This work addresses a key limitation in existing GUI agents, which are typically domain-specific and hinder deployment and user experience. By enabling effective consolidation into a single cross-environment policy, MAGA could significantly improve the practicality and scalability of GUI agents across mobile, web, and desktop platforms. MAGA operates on both the student and teacher sides, using a training-only hint to optimize supervision from domain-specific teachers without changing student input. It outperforms the strongest baseline by 2.0% at 8B scale and achieves nearly the same average performance as teachers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: GUI agents based on large language models are increasingly used across mobile, web, and desktop environments, but they are typically domain-specific. Weight merging can corrupt executable actions under expert disagreement, while on-policy distillation (OPD) treats all response tokens equally, ignoring that action tokens are the only interface between the environment and the agent. MAGA addresses this by focusing distillation on erroneous actions based on structured action correctness.

## [SESA: Self-Evolving Search Agents with Skill Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29468) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SESA (Self-Evolving Skill-Augmented Agent) is introduced as a novel framework that integrates procedural skill memory into tool-augmented search self-play, enabling a bidirectional loop where problem generation and skill memory co-evolve. It improves average accuracy by 1.2–3.2 points over SSP across seven benchmarks and surpasses SkillRL by 0.9 points. This work addresses a key limitation in self-play agents by making skill memory an evolving state rather than a static external component, which can improve learning efficiency and performance in search-based tasks. It has potential implications for AI/ML research, particularly in developing agents that can continuously adapt and improve without human intervention. SESA uses a challenger to pose problems and a separately parameterized solver to retrieve skills, with informative failures distilled into reusable skills and written back to memory. On Qwen3 models, SESA-Off retains 1.8–2.2 points improvement over SSP, and the final skill bank adds another 0.5–1.0 points, showing that skill memory benefits both policy learning and inference-time retrieval.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Self-play agents generate training problems without external questions, but their curricula lack persistent state, meaning failures do not explicitly shape future practice. External skill memories preserve procedural experience but are typically learned from fixed task distributions. SESA combines these by making procedural memory an evolving state within tool-augmented search self-play, allowing the agent to pose, solve, and remember in a continuous loop.

## [AMTFV: Agentic Mathematical Tool-Flow Verification for LLM Self-Correction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29549) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces AMTFV, a novel agentic verification framework that uses a Mathematical Tool Flow (MTF) as an interrupt-execute-resume interface to decouple verification modeling from concrete execution, enabling exact computation for LLM self-correction in mathematical tasks. Evaluated on five datasets with seven model configurations, it improves average accuracy over the strongest baseline by up to 8.3 percentage points. This work addresses a critical limitation in LLM mathematical reasoning: reliable verification of candidate answers. By decoupling modeling from execution and supporting exact computation, AMTFV enhances the reliability of LLM outputs, which is crucial for applications requiring high accuracy in math and science. It also introduces a promising agentic framework that could influence future verification methods. The MTF interface allows the verification agent to construct a verification workflow and encode mathematical objects and computational intent in an MTF request, which is then parsed by a mathematical toolbox agent that generates executable calls for exact computation. The framework supports candidate-answer adjudication, answer revision, and verification-workflow revision, and shows larger gains on samples of medium and high verification complexity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) have shown strong mathematical problem-solving abilities, but verifying their answers is challenging. Existing methods either use natural-language reflection, which may not support exact computation, or directly generate verification programs, which prematurely couples modeling with low-level implementation. AMTFV introduces a Mathematical Tool Flow (MTF) as an interrupt-execute-resume interface to decouple these concerns, enabling exact computation through a mathematical toolbox.

## [COntExt: Context-Aware Ontology Extension from Operational Metrics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29553) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces COntExt, a framework that automatically suggests ontology extensions from structured metric definitions, breaking the task into parent class prediction, relation type prediction, and data property assignment. It evaluates the framework across four cybersecurity ontologies, showing that metric-derived context improves suggestions for relation type prediction and data property assignment over ontology-context baselines. This work addresses a real gap in knowledge engineering by automating the labor-intensive process of ontology extension, potentially reducing maintenance costs for organizations. It also highlights operational metric catalogues as an underexploited source of domain knowledge, which could benefit fields like cybersecurity where ontologies are crucial for contextualizing threats. The framework defines three sub-tasks: parent class prediction, relation type prediction, and data property assignment, and evaluates different algorithms for each. The results demonstrate that using metric context improves performance on two of the three tasks, but the paper does not specify the exact algorithms or datasets used, nor does it provide quantitative performance metrics.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Ontologies are formal representations of domain knowledge, defining concepts, properties, and relationships. In cybersecurity, ontologies help contextualize threats and attacks, but maintaining them manually is costly. Operational metrics, defined in structured formats, implicitly encode domain knowledge that can extend ontologies, but this connection is often overlooked.

## [LEMUR: Multi-Objective RL from Preference Feedback](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29559) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LEMUR is a novel framework that integrates multi-objective reinforcement learning with preference-based feedback, allowing agents to learn policies and multiple objective-specific reward models simultaneously from human preferences. It addresses the gap where existing MORL methods require well-specified reward functions and PbRL is typically single-objective. This work is significant because it enables RL agents to handle competing objectives in real-world tasks without predefined reward functions, which is common in many applications. It could broaden the applicability of RL to domains where human preferences are easier to elicit than numerical rewards. LEMUR jointly learns policies and multiple objective-specific reward models from human feedback, and it is evaluated on benchmark multi-objective tasks, showing superior performance over baseline methods. The paper is an arXiv preprint (arXiv:2607.29559) and has not yet undergone peer review.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) trains agents to maximize a scalar reward, but many real-world tasks involve multiple competing objectives. Multi-objective RL (MORL) extends RL to vector rewards, but typically requires well-specified reward functions. Preference-based RL (PbRL) learns from human preferences instead of explicit rewards, but has been mostly studied in single-objective settings. LEMUR combines these two areas to learn multi-objective policies from preferences.

## [Four-World Framework Models Interpretive Perspectives in Computational Creativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28644) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a four-world framework to model interpretive perspectives in computational creativity, operationalized through three evaluative personas (formalist, social-historical, iconographic) applied to 1,069 artworks from the SemArt dataset, generating 38,484 persona-based evaluations. Results show systematic divergence across perspectives, with traits like Social Reflexivity exhibiting strong viewpoint sensitivity, and linear probing of CLIP embeddings reveals distinct orientation vectors for each persona. This work addresses a significant gap in computational creativity evaluation by shifting from objective, artifact-centric assessment to a relational, perspective-dependent view. It could enable co-creative AI systems to better support diverse human collaborators by accounting for interpretive differences, impacting fields like AI evaluation, art analysis, and human-AI interaction. The study uses a twelve-trait creativity framework organized across four conceptual domains, and employs linear probing of CLIP image embeddings to show that perspectives correspond to distinct orientation vectors in representation space. The findings suggest that creativity evaluation depends on which visual features become salient under each persona condition, supporting a relational view of creativity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Computational creativity (CC) is a multidisciplinary field at the intersection of AI, cognitive psychology, philosophy, and the arts, focusing on developing systems that can produce creative artifacts. Traditional CC evaluation frameworks often assess creativity as an objective property of outputs or systems, but artistic meaning is inherently perspective-dependent, varying across viewers and critical traditions. This paper proposes a computational approach to model interpretive perspectives, using personas to operationalize different evaluative viewpoints, and leverages the SemArt dataset and CLIP embeddings to empirically demonstrate perspective-dependent variability in creativity assessment.

## [GenAI in System Admin May Compress Expertise Pathways](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28650) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A qualitative study based on 14 semi-structured interviews with IT professionals reveals two unanticipated effects of generative AI (GenAI) in system administration: compression of traditional expertise pathways and a shift in performance perception. The paper, available on arXiv (2607.28650), uses inductive thematic analysis to describe how GenAI acts as a mentor-like tutor but also reduces hands-on learning opportunities. This research highlights a potential downside of GenAI adoption beyond productivity gains, raising concerns about the long-term development of technical expertise in high-stakes environments. It could influence how organizations balance AI assistance with preserving hands-on learning and how professional value is assessed. The study identifies a 'two-speed culture' within teams and 'productivity guilt' where manual work, even when necessary for safety, is perceived as slow. The findings suggest that GenAI can shorten the ladder to task completion but may reduce exposure to foundational cycles of building, failing, and debugging.

## [Federated Pre-Training Evaluation: Downstream Fine-Tuning Can Mislead](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28658) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a controlled study comparing evaluation protocols for federated pre-trained models, finding that downstream fine-tuning on GLUE does not reliably preserve the pre-training ranking, while intrinsic next-token prediction on GLUE text strongly correlates with pre-training test perplexity. This finding is significant because it challenges the common practice of using downstream fine-tuning to evaluate federated pre-trained models, which could lead to misleading conclusions. It suggests that evaluation signals closer to the pre-training objective, such as intrinsic next-token prediction, deserve more attention in federated learning research. The study uses a 16M parameter transformer model trained on identical client data, comparing centralized and federated training. Evaluation protocols include full, head-only, and reduced-data fine-tuning on GLUE, as well as next-token prediction on GLUE text as an intrinsic signal.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Federated pre-training allows training foundation models on private or distributed data without centralizing datasets. However, evaluating such models is challenging because client participation and local data availability vary, and pre-training test perplexity is tied to the pre-training distribution. Downstream benchmarks introduce task-specific adaptation that may not reflect pre-training quality.

## [Study Compares GRU, LSTM, Transformer for ADS Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28665) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper evaluates GRU, LSTM, and Transformer encoder models for classifying Level 2 automated driving systems (Comma Openpilot, Tesla Autopilot, Cadillac Super Cruise) and manual driving from vehicle telematics data. All models achieve high macro F1-scores (0.90-0.93) on clean data, and a robustness framework reveals that temporal jitter severely degrades performance (macro F1 drops to 0.44-0.50). This work addresses a novel and practical problem: independently verifying which automated driving system is active, which is crucial for safety monitoring, regulatory compliance, insurance assessment, and anomaly detection. The findings highlight the vulnerability of sequence models to temporal jitter, informing future robust model design for real-world telematics. The robustness evaluation framework simulates five corruption families at five severity levels (L1-L5), including additive white Gaussian noise with cumulative drift, correlated cross-channel noise, temporal jitter, and event-level corruptions like burst loss and spurious toggles. Event-level corruptions cause only slight performance drops (macro F1 ≥ 0.87 at L5), while temporal jitter collapses macro F1 to 0.44-0.50 across all models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Automated driving systems (ADSs) are becoming common, and future Software Defined Vehicles (SDVs) may run multiple ADSs, both native and aftermarket. Sequence-based deep learning models like GRU, LSTM, and Transformer encoders are widely used for time-series classification, but their robustness to real-world data corruption is not well understood. This paper introduces a modular framework to evaluate such robustness, using macro-F1 to handle imbalanced multiclass classification.

## [LAWFUL: A Framework to Verify Neural Networks Learn and Use Physical Laws](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28672) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces LAWFUL, a framework that addresses four interpretability gaps in verifying whether neural networks learn and use physics laws over continuous variables. It is demonstrated on the Mocap2Radar transformer to validate whether it learns and internally uses the Doppler frequency law f(t) = 2v(t)/λ. This framework provides a rigorous method for interpretability in physics-informed machine learning, potentially improving trust and reliability in AI systems used for scientific discovery and engineering. It addresses a critical gap in verifying that models not only predict accurately but also reason according to known physical laws. LAWFUL closes the first two gaps: a coverage-aware causal-consistency measure over continuous counterfactuals and a domain-of-validity test for the identified circuit. It lays groundwork for the remaining two gaps: verification of the law's invariants and forbidden behaviors, and quantification of how a derived physical quantity flows through the circuit.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Neural networks are often used to predict physical systems, but it is unclear whether they learn the underlying physical laws as structured knowledge. The Doppler effect describes the change in frequency of a wave due to relative motion, and the formula f(t) = 2v(t)/λ relates Doppler frequency to velocity and wavelength. The Mocap2Radar transformer is a model that synthesizes radar spectrograms from motion-capture data, and this paper uses it as a case study.

## [MPP-GNN: Bilevel Optimization for Adaptive fMRI Brain Module Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28681) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MPP-GNN, a graph neural network that uses bilevel optimization to adaptively discover subject-specific brain modules for Alzheimer's disease classification from fMRI data, achieving the highest AUC on two public datasets. This work addresses the critical issue of inter-subject variability in brain functional modules, which is often overlooked in existing GNN-based methods. By improving classification performance and aligning with canonical brain networks, it could enhance diagnostic accuracy and provide insights into Alzheimer's disease progression. MPP-GNN frames the task as a coupled bilevel optimization that hierarchically partitions graphs to discover modules, then uses these modules as an explicit prior to guide edge refinement and representation learning. The model demonstrates significant alignment with the Yeo brain atlas and reveals a network-level dedifferentiation pattern for AD.

## [Metaphors Can Steer LLM Code Generation Toward Inefficient Algorithms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28683) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper introduces 'metaphorical algorithmic steering,' showing that metaphorical instructions can cause LLMs to transfer procedural patterns from source domains, leading to less efficient algorithm choices in code generation. The authors developed the MASC framework to elicit and study this phenomenon. This finding highlights a subtle risk in prompt engineering: seemingly benign metaphors can inadvertently degrade code quality, impacting AI safety and reliability. It underscores the need for careful prompt design in code-generation tasks and opens new avenues for understanding and mitigating algorithmic bias in LLMs. The MASC framework iteratively metaphorizes and refines benign skills to elicit low-efficiency code while remaining task-relevant. The study also shows that metaphorical skills induce a hidden-state shift toward lower-efficiency procedural prototypes, and the method achieves high detection rates for such skills.

This review synthesizes recent technological advances, including EEG, MRI, blood-based biomarkers, and digital markers, integrated with AI/ML/DL for detecting cognitive impairment in older adults. It introduces a taxonomy and an early-detection framework linking tiered screening to intervention. As populations age, early detection of cognitive decline is critical, and this review highlights promising tools like plasma p-tau217 and AI models that could improve diagnosis and management. It underscores the need for rigorous external validation to ensure these technologies are reliable and equitable in clinical practice. EEG markers (alpha/theta changes, P300 latency) and deep models (CNNs, LSTM/BiLSTM, transformers) report high accuracy but often rely on small, single-site datasets. Plasma p-tau217 has reached clinical utility, with the first blood test cleared for Alzheimer's diagnosis in 2025, and anti-amyloid therapies (lecanemab, donanemab) are approved despite modest benefits.

## [Reflected UAS Routing: Corrected Stability and Direct CTMC Drift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28688) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper corrects the stability analysis of Reflected UAS routing in heterogeneous multi-server queues by introducing a reflected ODE model and a direct Foster-Lyapunov drift inequality for the CTMC. It shows that the previous deterministic lift fails and provides a new proof of stability. This work fills a theoretical gap in queueing theory and routing policy analysis, providing a rigorous foundation for Reflected UAS. It could influence future research on stochastic stability and the design of efficient routing policies in heterogeneous systems. The reflected ODE has a unique boundary equilibrium characterized by a scalar consistency equation and a convex-potential representation. At the benchmark parameter point, the boundary equilibrium matches the numerical attractor to machine precision, and the default Reflected UAS policy achieves lower mean queue length than UAS and JSSQ.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Reflected UAS (Utility-based Allocation Strategy) is a routing policy for multi-server queues where each server has a utility function, and tasks are routed to the server with the highest marginal utility. The stability of such policies is often analyzed using deterministic ODE approximations and Foster-Lyapunov drift conditions for the underlying continuous-time Markov chain (CTMC). This paper addresses a flaw in previous deterministic-to-stochastic lifting arguments.

## [PINNs for Geometric Analysis: Lessons from the Asymptotic Plateau Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28733) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This proceedings paper presents methodological lessons from using physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) to construct near-minimal discs in hyperbolic space asymptotic to a prescribed knot, providing numerical evidence for a conjecture by Joel Fine relating minimal surfaces in H^4 to HOMFLY polynomial coefficients. The paper details two implementation techniques that reduce training step cost by a factor of 40-50. This work demonstrates a novel application of PINNs to a geometric analysis problem, offering a blueprint for encoding geometric constraints into neural network architectures. It provides valuable methodological insights for researchers at the intersection of machine learning and geometry, potentially enabling broader use of PINNs in differential geometry and geometric analysis. The paper emphasizes two critical aspects: encoding the geometry of the problem into the model architecture so that boundary conditions and asymptotics hold exactly, and engineering the PDE residual evaluation for efficiency. The two implementation techniques are replacing nested reverse-mode automatic differentiation with forward propagation of second-order jets, and compiling the computational graph of the residual once instead of rebuilding it at each optimization step.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) are a class of deep learning models that embed physical laws, typically expressed as partial differential equations (PDEs), into the training process, acting as a regularization agent. The asymptotic Plateau problem in hyperbolic space asks for the existence of an area-minimizing submanifold asymptotic to a given submanifold at infinity. The HOMFLY polynomial is a two-variable knot invariant that generalizes the Alexander and Jones polynomials, and Joel Fine's conjecture relates its coefficients to minimal surfaces in H^4.

## [SCMA: Structure-Conditioned Flow Matching for CT Metal Artifact Reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28759) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SCMA, a novel Flow Matching framework that incorporates structure conditioning and metal-aware weighting to reduce metal artifacts in CT images. It alternates conditional Flow Matching with projection-consistency correction during inference, improving anatomical consistency and generalization. This work addresses key limitations of existing MAR methods, such as residual artifacts, poor generalization, and anatomical inconsistency, potentially improving diagnostic accuracy in CT imaging with metallic implants. It advances the application of generative models in medical imaging, offering a more reliable approach for clinical use. SCMA uses a linear-interpolation-corrected image as a structural condition for the velocity network, and incorporates time-varying spatial weights derived from the metal mask and its distance transform into the Flow Matching loss. Experiments on simulated and real CT data show that SCMA suppresses artifacts, preserves local structures, and reduces hallucination-like structures compared to representative MAR methods.

Flow Matching is a generative modeling technique that learns a continuous-time velocity field to transport a source distribution to a target distribution, providing a flexible prior for image generation. ## [Preference-Optimized LLM Counselors Trade Goal Persistence for Relational Attunement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28814) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a two-axis evaluation framework (Goal Persistence and Relational Attunement) for counselor responses in Motivational Interviewing, and empirically shows that penalizing confrontation through preference optimization lowers goal persistence across three LLM families, while attunement gains are inconsistent. This work highlights a critical trade-off in AI alignment for therapeutic contexts: optimizing for one failure mode (confrontation) can inadvertently harm another desirable quality (goal persistence). It provides a framework for evaluating and improving LLM-based counseling, with implications for AI safety and human-AI interaction. The study uses the AnnoMI corpus to build Direct Preference Optimization (DPO) data with on-policy negatives, and employs a firewall where disjoint model families generate, label, and judge. Penalizing capitulation was inert because models rarely capitulate on-policy, and a prompt-only control raised attunement without the goal-persistence cost.

**Background**: Motivational Interviewing (MI) is a counseling approach that addresses ambivalence about change, where 'rolling with resistance' is a key technique. The Motivational Interviewing Treatment Integrity (MITI) code is a standard tool for evaluating MI fidelity. AnnoMI is a publicly available corpus of MI conversations annotated with expert labels. Preference optimization, such as DPO, is a method to align LLMs with human preferences by training on preference pairs.

<a id="item-169"></a>
## [Hessian-Free Bilevel RL Achieves State-of-the-Art Sample Complexity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28849) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a Hessian-free hypergradient-based bilevel RL algorithm that achieves an iteration complexity of O(ε⁻¹) and a state-of-the-art sample complexity of Õ(ε⁻²) under mild regularity conditions. It also removes the Polyak-Lojasiewicz (PL) condition assumption on the outer-level objective, which was required in prior work. This work addresses two major limitations in bilevel RL: scalability issues from Hessian-based hypergradients and high sample complexity from penalty-based methods. By achieving Hessian-free computation and improved sample complexity without the PL condition, it broadens the applicability of bilevel RL to meta-learning, RLHF, and hierarchical RL, potentially accelerating progress in these areas. The algorithm leverages the optimality of the Boltzmann policy for the entropy-regularized discounted RL objective to compute hypergradients without Hessian matrices. The convergence analysis removes the PL condition, which is a notable theoretical contribution, and the sample complexity matches or improves upon prior state-of-the-art results.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Bilevel reinforcement learning (RL) formalizes problems with two intertwined levels, such as meta-learning, hierarchical task decomposition, and RL from human feedback (RLHF). Traditional bilevel RL algorithms either rely on Hessian-based hypergradients, which are computationally expensive, or use penalty-based approximations that suffer from high sample complexity. The Polyak-Lojasiewicz condition is a strong assumption that ensures favorable convergence properties but is often hard to verify in practice. This paper proposes a Hessian-free approach that avoids these issues and achieves better theoretical guarantees.

## [Unified Benchmark of 3D Deep Learning Models for Brain Tumor Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28858) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a unified experimental benchmark that evaluates five state-of-the-art 3D deep learning models—3D U-Net, SegResNet, Swin UNETR, SegMamba, and SegMambaV2—for brain tumor segmentation under identical preprocessing, augmentation, optimization, and evaluation protocols across two MRI datasets (BraTS 2023 and BraTS 2024). This benchmark addresses the critical issue of inconsistent evaluation in medical image segmentation, providing a fair comparison that helps researchers and clinicians choose appropriate models based on accuracy and computational efficiency. It also highlights the trade-offs between different architectural paradigms (CNNs, Transformers, and SSMs), guiding future model development and clinical deployment. The models are evaluated on two distinct clinical scenarios: intracranial meningioma segmentation (BraTS 2023) and post-treatment glioma segmentation (BraTS 2024). Performance is measured using segmentation accuracy metrics along with computational cost indicators such as inference time and model size, offering practical insights into efficiency-accuracy trade-offs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Brain tumor segmentation from MRI is essential for diagnosis and treatment planning, but comparing deep learning models is difficult due to varied experimental setups. This benchmark unifies conditions to fairly compare CNNs (e.g., 3D U-Net, SegResNet), Transformers (Swin UNETR), and State Space Models (SegMamba, SegMambaV2), which represent recent advances in 3D medical image analysis.

<a id="item-171"></a>
## [TextCloak: RL-Driven Unlearnable Text to Thwart Unauthorized LLM Exploitation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28862) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TextCloak introduces a reinforcement learning-driven framework that generates unlearnable text examples, preserving semantics while degrading LLM performance when trained on protected data. It uses GRPO-UE, a group-relative policy optimization reward, to optimize a generative policy for open-ended generation settings. This work addresses the critical issue of unauthorized data exploitation in the LLM era, offering a practical defense that extends unlearnable examples beyond classification tasks to open-ended generation. It could significantly impact privacy and data security, providing a tool for individuals and organizations to protect their textual data from being effectively used in unauthorized LLM fine-tuning. TextCloak employs a bi-level optimization: a generative policy transforms clean text into unlearnable examples, and GRPO-UE rewards based on downstream degradation in fine-tuned surrogate LLMs. Experiments on six datasets and nine LLMs show consistent impairment of unauthorized fine-tuning while maintaining utility, with demonstrated transferability and robustness across architectures and adaptive attacks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Unlearnable examples (UEs) are a defense mechanism that adds carefully designed perturbations to data, causing models trained on them to have degraded performance. Previous text-based UE methods were limited to classification tasks and relied on class-specific cues, which are ineffective for open-ended generation in LLMs. TextCloak overcomes this by using reinforcement learning to learn generalizable protective patterns.

<a id="item-172"></a>
## [RareSense: Rarity-Aware Similarity Search for Anomaly Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28879) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RareSense is a new framework that mines minimal rare itemsets and rare association rules to build sparse rule profiles, then uses weighted Jaccard similarity for anomaly retrieval. It is shown to generalize IDF-weighted Jaccard as a special case and outperforms atomic baselines in benchmark experiments. This addresses a key limitation of classical similarity measures in sparse transactional data, where frequent background attributes dominate. It offers a theoretically grounded approach that improves anomaly detection and retrieval, potentially benefiting cybersecurity and other categorical data mining tasks. RareSense uses rule weights combining inverse support, confidence, lift, structural complexity, and stability. The induced distance is a pseudometric on original objects and a metric over equivalence classes of identical rule profiles; experiments show workload-dependent gains, strongest when anomalies share repeatable rare higher-order structure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Similarity search over sparse set-valued data often relies on measures like Jaccard, cosine, and Hamming, which compare objects via atomic overlap. IDF weighting partially mitigates frequent attribute dominance but cannot capture higher-order co-occurrences. Rare itemsets and rare association rules are data mining concepts that focus on infrequent but potentially informative patterns, which RareSense leverages.

## [Human-LLM Collaborative Coding Pipeline for K-12 Educator AI Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28889) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a multi-phase human-LLM collaborative coding pipeline that adapts open, axial, and selective coding to develop a hierarchical codebook from 45,000 educator-AI messages, with human researchers retaining conceptual authority. The final codebook comprises 72 items within 19 categories and six domains, and was validated through systematic human coding on an independent sample of 2,560 messages. This work addresses the growing challenge of analyzing large-scale qualitative interaction corpora, offering a scalable and auditable template for integrating LLMs into qualitative research while preserving human interpretive authority. It provides practical insights for researchers in AI-assisted analysis, particularly in education and other domains facing similar data volume issues. The pipeline treats the LLM as a labeling instrument rather than an interpretive agent, and uses set-valued agreement measures for multi-label annotation during reliability testing. Three trained coders extended the codebook with five additional codes that the LLM-assisted phases did not surface, highlighting the continued importance of human domain expertise.

**Background**: Qualitative research traditionally involves manual coding of data, but the scale of modern interaction corpora often exceeds manual capacity. ## [DiffAttack: Latent Diffusion-Based Adversarial Faces Evade FR Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28936) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces DiffAttack, a novel framework that uses latent diffusion models to generate adversarial faces via latent-space optimization, achieving an average attack success rate of 84.86% across multiple face recognition models like FaceNet. It outperforms existing noise-based and semantic-based methods by over 15.28% and approximately 5.21% respectively on FFHQ and CelebA-HQ benchmarks. This work addresses critical limitations in adversarial attacks on face recognition, particularly cross-demographic and cross-gender transferability and image quality, which are crucial for real-world biometric security. It demonstrates the potential of generative models in crafting imperceptible yet effective attacks, highlighting vulnerabilities in current FR systems and informing future defense strategies. DiffAttack leverages latent diffusion models to directly guide generation toward target identity embeddings, as measured by a face recognition model. It was evaluated on FFHQ and CelebA-HQ datasets, showing superior transferability compared to traditional methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Face recognition systems map faces to high-dimensional embeddings, and adversarial attacks craft imperceptible perturbations to fool these systems. Latent diffusion models (LDMs) are generative models that perform diffusion in a compressed latent space, enabling high-quality image synthesis. Existing adversarial methods often produce low-quality images or fail across demographic groups, which DiffAttack aims to overcome.

## [Training-Free Retrieval Method for AI-Generated Video Attribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28955) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a training-free, retrieval-driven method for attributing AI-generated videos to their source models, using a generative fingerprint pipeline with orthogonal color transformation, multi-scale quantized residual generation, and temporal-semantic aggregation. On the GenVidBench benchmark, it achieves a Rank-1 accuracy of 20.5% and a mean Average Precision of 16.6%, outperforming existing state-of-the-art methods. This work addresses the growing threat of realistic AI-generated videos by enabling forensic attribution to specific generative models, which is crucial for cybersecurity and legal regulation. It extends image attribution methods to video, a challenging and underexplored area, potentially aiding deepfake detection and content provenance. The method is training-free, meaning it does not require fine-tuning on video data, and it formulates attribution as an instance retrieval task. The pipeline captures and aggregates generation artifacts across frames using adapted orthogonal color transformation, multi-scale quantized residuals, and temporal-semantic aggregation, with code available on GitHub.

## [Efficient LLM Adversarial Training via Low-Rank Defense and Circuit-Guided Surrogates](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28959) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes two complementary strategies to speed up latent adversarial training (LAT) for large language models: defense-side optimization using representation fine-tuning (ReFT) and attack-side optimization using circuit-guided surrogate models. The method reduces per-step adversarial-training FLOPs by 48.1% on average while requiring only 0.0118% trainable parameters compared to standard LAT with full fine-tuning. Adversarial training is a key defense against adversarial attacks, but its high computational cost limits its application to large language models. By significantly reducing computational overhead, this work makes adversarial training more practical for LLMs, potentially improving their robustness in real-world deployments. The defense-side optimization uses representation fine-tuning (ReFT), which modifies less than 1% of model representations, and the paper reveals a potential issue when there is a mismatch between the tokens used for ReFT and the attack. The attack-side optimization extracts only relevant circuits from the LLM to build a lightweight surrogate model, avoiding full-model forward-backward passes during attack generation. The paper provides theoretical justifications and numerical evidence for both strategies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Adversarial training is a defense method that involves training a model on adversarial examples to improve its robustness. Latent adversarial training (LAT) is a variant that perturbs intermediate representations rather than inputs, but it still incurs high computational costs. Representation fine-tuning (ReFT) is a parameter-efficient technique that modifies only a small fraction of model representations, and circuit analysis identifies the specific neural pathways responsible for certain behaviors, enabling the construction of lightweight surrogates.

## [RAID: Robust AI-Generated Image Detection via Bit-Reversed Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28974) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces RAID, a novel AI-generated image detection method that leverages bit-reversed images, gradient-based patch selection, and a convolutional classifier. It also provides theoretical analysis and introduces two new challenging datasets, achieving superior performance on over 40 benchmarks while being nearly 100 times faster than existing methods. This work addresses the critical need for robust and generalizable AI-generated image detection, which is essential for mitigating the misuse of deepfakes and synthetic media. By introducing a novel bit-plane perspective and achieving state-of-the-art performance with high efficiency, RAID could significantly enhance real-world detection systems and influence future research in this domain. The method constructs bit-reversed images by reversing the bit order of pixel values, then selects informative patches using gradient-based criteria, and classifies them with a convolutional neural network. The theoretical analysis provides mathematical justification for the approach, and the new datasets are designed to challenge existing detectors with more diverse and realistic AI-generated images.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: AI-generated image detection is a growing field aimed at distinguishing synthetic images from real ones, driven by the rapid advancement of generative models. Traditional methods often rely on visual artifacts or frequency-domain features, but they may lack robustness across different generators and datasets. Bit-plane analysis, which examines the binary representation of pixel values, offers a novel perspective that can reveal subtle statistical differences between real and fake images.

## [PARALLEL: Prefrontal-Aligned Reinforcement-Inspired Efficient Language Model Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28982) ⭐️ 7.0/10

PARALLEL introduces a novel adaptation method that uses a reinforcement-inspired controller to assign sample-dependent update intensity, learning when and how strongly to update each sample. It retains 94.1-99.2% of full-adaptation performance while reducing unnecessary parameter changes. This approach addresses the inefficiency of uniform updates in language model adaptation, offering a more stable and energy-efficient alternative for post-deployment stream adaptation. It could enable more sustainable and targeted fine-tuning in real-world applications, especially where computational resources are limited. PARALLEL combines goal-related and uncertainty-related control signals as separate controller inputs, and uses immediate utility-cost feedback to guide updates. Experiments on multiple-choice reasoning and summarization (XSum, CNN/DailyMail) show it retains 96.9-98.9% of ROUGE scores, and achieves higher ARC accuracy at the same cumulative adaptation time or GPU energy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Language model adaptation typically applies uniform updates across all training samples, which can be inefficient. Reinforcement learning (RL) involves agents learning from delayed scalar feedback (rewards) to make sequential decisions. PARALLEL draws inspiration from the prefrontal cortex's complementary roles in goal-related and uncertainty-related control, and applies an RL-inspired controller to decide update intensity per sample.

## [Adjudicated Captioning: Multi-Agent Framework Boosts Zero-Shot Image Captioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28986) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Adjudicated Captioning, an inference-time multi-agent framework that improves zero-shot image captioning by adding retrieval re-ranking, cross-attention verification, and a learned reranker to the IFCap captioner. It achieves a CIDEr of 117.6 on COCO Karpathy, up from 108.0 for IFCap, a +9.6 gain. This work addresses a known limitation in zero-shot image captioning—the lack of visual grounding feedback during decoding—and demonstrates significant improvements over the state-of-the-art without retraining the captioner. It could inspire further research into inference-time multi-agent approaches for vision-language tasks. The framework includes a stronger frozen Retrieval Encoder, a frozen Cross-Attention Verifier that re-ranks top-9 retrievals to top-5, and a learned Reranker combining TriFuse (MLP) and MemAttend (memory-attended transformer), trained via Borda-consensus distillation without paired labels. The training-free fixed-fusion baseline reaches 115.8 CIDEr, indicating most gains come from architectural interventions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Zero-shot image captioning (ZIC) aims to describe images without paired image-caption supervision, relying on text-only corpora and frozen pretrained image-text scorers. IFCap is a prior method that uses image-like retrieval and frequency-based entity filtering for zero-shot captioning. Existing retrieval-augmented methods typically score image-text alignment once at retrieval and then rely solely on language model probability during decoding, lacking further visual grounding feedback.

This paper introduces a sparsity-biased classifier-free guidance (SB-CFG) strategy for diffusion models, which uses a deliberately under-informative sparse reference for the unconditional branch to improve conditional scRNA-seq generation. Evaluated on five public scRNA-seq datasets, SB-CFG consistently outperforms standard CFG in marker gene expression fidelity, cell-type consistency, and sparsity preservation. This work addresses a key limitation of existing guidance methods in single-cell data generation, potentially improving the quality and biological fidelity of synthetic scRNA-seq data. It offers a training-free modification that can be applied to existing diffusion models, benefiting researchers in cellular biology and computational biology. SB-CFG is a training-free sampling modification that replaces the unconditional branch's approximation of the marginal distribution with a sparse reference that removes gene identity while preserving coarse sparsity statistics. The method is inspired by recent work on using intentionally degraded references for guidance and is evaluated on five publicly available scRNA-seq datasets.

A new study benchmarked six frontier LLMs on coding crash attributes from police narratives against official Arkansas fatal-crash records, finding that GPT-5.5 High achieved the highest agreement among LLMs, but simple baselines like always-majority and keyword rules performed comparably or better. This work highlights the potential of LLMs to automate crash database coding, which could reduce manual labor and improve traffic safety data processing. The study linked 5,587 fatal-crash narratives with 5,889 structured records (2015-2025), yielding 4,194 matched crashes. Performance was measured using agreement, macro-averaged F1, Cohen's kappa, coverage, and selective agreement, with repeated-measures and GEE analyses. Agreement was highest for non-motorist relation and crash manner, lowest for light condition, roadway surface, and work-zone relation.

## [Legal Deception Detection: Fine-Tuned Models vs. Few-Shot LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29066) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper benchmarks deception detection in legal contexts, comparing six fine-tuned transformer models and seven LLMs across seven datasets, and finds that fine-tuned models excel in general domains while few-shot LLMs are competitive in low-resource legal settings. It also reveals that Chain-of-Thought prompting often underperforms direct classification. This research is significant for NLP and legal tech, as it highlights the domain sensitivity of deception detection models and the need for domain adaptation in high-stakes legal contexts. The findings on Chain-of-Thought prompting challenge common assumptions and could influence future model design and prompting strategies. The evaluation covers two legal datasets and five general-domain datasets, using six fine-tuned transformer models and seven LLMs under four prompting strategies. The results show strong domain sensitivity, with fine-tuned models performing better in data-rich general domains, while few-shot LLMs remain competitive in low-resource legal settings.

**Background**: Deception detection is critical in legal proceedings, law enforcement, and online security, but human judgment is limited in accuracy and scalability. NLP-based Automatic Deception Detection (ADD) has evolved from feature-based machine learning to Large Language Model (LLM) approaches, offering a data-driven alternative. Fine-tuning adapts a pretrained model to a specific task with labeled data, while few-shot learning allows LLMs to generalize from just a few examples. Chain-of-Thought prompting is a technique that instructs the model to generate intermediate reasoning steps, but it may not always improve performance.

## [DoubleHelix: Iterative Cross-Modal Fusion for AVSR Achieves 0.68% WER](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29112) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DoubleHelix introduces a novel framework for audio-visual speech recognition (AVSR) that reformulates fusion as an iterative cross-modal interaction process with degradation-aware enhancement. It achieves a state-of-the-art 0.68% word error rate (WER) on the LRS3 benchmark under clean audio conditions. This work pushes the state of the art in AVSR, demonstrating the benefit of structured iterative fusion over single-step approaches. It could influence future multimodal systems that rely on robust fusion under noisy conditions, such as voice assistants and video conferencing tools. The framework comprises three components: ReverseParallelHelix for multi-turn structured interaction with learned alignment constraints, QualitySensor for degradation-aware gating signals, and HelixReplication for consistency-guided conditional feature enhancement. It also shows improved robustness under babble noise, achieving 11.6% WER at SNR -5dB.

**Background**: Audio-visual speech recognition (AVSR) combines audio and visual signals to improve recognition accuracy, especially in noisy environments. Traditional methods often treat cross-modal fusion as a single-step operation, which may not fully exploit the complementary information. DoubleHelix addresses this by introducing iterative refinement and degradation-aware mechanisms, leveraging pre-trained models like Whisper and AV-HuBERT.

## [MGLP: Multi-Granularity Position Embedding for Link Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29115) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes MGLP, a novel method that uses adaptive granular-ball graph refinement to obtain multi-granularity position embeddings for link prediction, and introduces a multi-granularity hierarchical distance encoding mechanism. This work addresses a gap in existing link prediction methods by capturing the multi-granularity nature of homophilic structures and their hierarchical interrelations, potentially improving the discriminative power of nodes and advancing graph neural network research. MGLP introduces an Adaptive Granular-Ball Graph Refinement mechanism that iteratively decomposes the graph into homophilic subdomains with optimal granularity, and the central nodes of these subdomains form a Hierarchical Central Graph. The method also proposes a Multi-granularity Hierarchical Distance encoding mechanism to capture both homophilic structures and their hierarchical correlations.

**Background**: Link prediction aims to identify potential or future connections in a graph. Position information is crucial for distinguishing homogeneous nodes, and previous methods often use distances to single-granularity landmarks, neglecting the multi-granularity nature of homophilic structures. Granular-ball graph refinement is a technique that adaptively aggregates coherent regions into supernodes, which MGLP leverages to create multi-granularity embeddings.

## [InferQ: Database-Oriented Benchmark for Quantum Circuit Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29134) ⭐️ 7.0/10

InferQ is a new benchmark that generates compositional quantum circuits and compiles them into SQL workloads, releasing a dataset of 202,975 circuits with a web viewer. It provides static, graph, SQL, and dynamic features for workload characterization. This bridges quantum computing and database systems, enabling systematic research in query optimization and physical design for SQL-based quantum simulation. It shows RDBMSs can outperform Qiskit Aer in memory on over 50% of circuits, and ML models can predict when SQL is preferable with high accuracy. The benchmark includes circuit templates and feature extraction, and experiments across PostgreSQL, SQLite, DuckDB, and Umbra. Lightweight ML models achieved up to 95.3% accuracy for runtime and 97.4% for memory prediction.

## [ActFovea: Runtime Safeguarding for VLA Policies via Spatiotemporal Visual-Action Consistency](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29169) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ActFovea is a plug-and-play safeguarding framework that detects and mitigates temporal misalignments in vision-language-action (VLA) policies without retraining or modifying the underlying policy. In closed-loop evaluations of π0 across LIBERO suites, it increased success under localized visual overlays from 49.3% to 90.3%, closing 93.7% of the gap to clean performance. This addresses a critical runtime robustness issue for VLA policies, which are increasingly used in robotic manipulation but are vulnerable to disturbances like visual delays or action drift. By providing a non-invasive safeguarding layer, ActFovea could enhance the reliability and safety of deploying VLA systems in real-world applications, potentially accelerating their adoption in industry and research. ActFovea uses robot kinematics, proprioceptive states, and recent actions to construct action-conditioned foveated regions, retaining contact-relevant areas and predicted motion corridors while suppressing task-irrelevant visual content. It detects risks by evaluating whether visual motion and observation freshness align with geometric, proprioceptive, and action transitions, and for recoverable disturbances, it constructs candidate observations and verifies the resulting action chunk before acceptance. Under frozen-observation replay, it triggered timely safe failure in all trials with no unprotected failures.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language-action (VLA) policies combine visual perception, language understanding, and action generation to control robots, often using large pre-trained models like π0 (Pi-Zero) from Physical Intelligence. These policies achieve strong performance in manipulation tasks but can fail at runtime due to disturbances that break temporal alignment among observations, states, and actions. Foveated vision, inspired by human retinas, focuses processing on a small high-resolution region, which ActFovea adapts to prioritize task-relevant visual areas. The framework leverages spatiotemporal consistency—ensuring that visual motion and observation freshness match expected action transitions—to detect and recover from such failures.

## [SAF Framework Stabilizes RLVR and On-Policy Distillation Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29209) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SAF, a Stable Advantage Fusion framework that combines RLVR and on-policy distillation by sparsifying and compressing OPD advantages to prevent entropy collapse. It outperforms fixed-coefficient fusion across seven benchmarks with Qwen3 models, improving aggregate scores by 0.51-2.70%. This work addresses a critical challenge in combining RLVR and on-policy distillation, which are complementary but suffer from entropy collapse when naively fused. SAF provides a practical, lightweight solution that enables surpassing teacher performance, potentially improving LLM alignment and reasoning capabilities. SAF uses a four-stage pipeline applied only to the OPD advantage: sparsify-then-compress for magnitude control and warm-up-then-anneal for temporal control, with each stage independently switchable. The method is evaluated with GRPO on Qwen3-1.7B/4B/8B across mathematical reasoning and code generation benchmarks, showing stable training and consistent improvements.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: RLVR broadcasts a single response-level reward to all tokens, while on-policy distillation (OPD) provides dense token-level advantages from a stronger teacher but caps performance at teacher quality. The advantage function in RL measures the relative benefit of an action compared to the average, guiding policy updates. Fusing these advantages naively can cause entropy collapse due to magnitude and temporal mismatches, which SAF aims to resolve.

## [RecHarness: Bandit-Routed Agentic Framework for Recommender Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29241) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RecHarness introduces a bandit-routed agentic framework that separates direction selection from LLM-generated hypotheses, enabling stable self-evolving optimization of recommender models. In a 7-day online A/B test on a large-scale short-video advertising platform, it improved ADVV by 2.084%, Revenue by 0.534%, and Exposure by 0.559%. This work addresses the instability of LLM-driven automated machine learning by decoupling exploration direction from hypothesis generation, which could lead to more reliable and efficient recommender system optimization. It offers a practical solution that reduces manual engineering effort and improves performance in real-world advertising platforms. RecHarness uses a bandit router to select modification directions based on historical validation feedback, while the LLM generates concrete hypotheses and code edits within that direction. It also employs a jump-basin mechanism to activate a structural-jump arm when local edits stagnate, sustaining long-horizon exploration. The code is available at https://github.com/6lyc/RecHarness.

**Background**: Recommender system optimization often requires manual iteration over architectural and training changes. LLM-based agents can automate this process, but allowing the LLM to both select directions and generate hypotheses can lead to unstable search. Bandit algorithms provide a principled way to balance exploration and exploitation, and agentic frameworks orchestrate LLM-driven actions.

## [CalibratedRubric: Adaptive Rubric Banks for LLM Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29252) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CalibratedRubric introduces a task-adaptive framework that combines Bayesian measurability filtering and item response theory (IRT)-based selection to construct compact rubric banks for open-ended LLM evaluation. It improves human-gold agreement on JudgmentBench from κ=0.604 to 0.743 and reduces the number of rubrics needed on FinResearchBench from 131 to 49. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in LLM evaluation—the high cost and difficulty of scaling expert-curated rubrics. By automating rubric generation with principled statistical methods, it enables more reliable and efficient evaluation across diverse domains, benefiting both researchers and practitioners. The framework uses a Beta-Bernoulli agreement posterior to estimate rubric measurability and a submodular information-coverage objective for bank assembly. It also incorporates task-label perturbations to test robustness, and its gains depend on sufficient judge redundancy.

**Background**: Open-ended LLM evaluation often relies on fine-grained rubrics, but expert curation is costly and hard to scale. Existing automated pipelines use strict judge unanimity and binary variance filters, which fail to distinguish measurable rubrics from informative ones. CalibratedRubric leverages Bayesian methods and item response theory, a psychometric framework for modeling latent traits, to address this gap.

It underscores the need for robustness evaluation against structured cross-task semantic attacks, as perturbations transfer from classification to captioning and VQA. QR-STT uses a three-stage gradient-free procedure with greedy module-flip refinement to handle mixed discrete and continuous search spaces, optimizing position, scale, rotation, intensity, blur, and roundness. Experiments on multiple CLIP-style encoders show consistent redirection of image-text alignment while maintaining visual stealth, and perturbations transfer to captioning and VQA tasks.

Black-box attacks operate without access to model internals, often using gradient-free optimization.

The paper introduces CrossRAG, a retrieval-augmented forecasting framework that integrates Shape-Aware Memory (SAM) with RevIN normalization for magnitude-robust shape-level retrieval, Future-Consistent Contrastive (FCC) learning, and Cross-Attention Temporal Fusion (CATF) to fuse retrieved historical-future reference pairs into the backbone's representations. Experiments on seven public benchmarks show that CrossRAG consistently outperforms both parametric-only baselines and existing retrieval-augmented forecasting methods. By improving forecasting accuracy, CrossRAG could enhance resource scheduling and predictive maintenance in IoT and other large-scale multivariate time series applications. CrossRAG uses Shape-Aware Memory (SAM) with RevIN normalization for magnitude-robust shape-level retrieval, Future-Consistent Contrastive (FCC) learning to distinguish informative references from hard negatives, and Cross-Attention Temporal Fusion (CATF) to fuse retrieved pairs at the representation level. The framework is evaluated on seven public benchmarks, demonstrating consistent improvements over baselines.

**Background**: Time series forecasting is critical for applications like resource scheduling and predictive maintenance, especially with large-scale multivariate data from IoT sensors. Recent foundation models rely on static parametric knowledge and lack dynamic access to external historical patterns during inference. Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) offers a way to incorporate external knowledge, but its application to time series faces challenges such as magnitude variations and history-future mismatch. RevIN is a normalization technique that addresses distribution shift in time series, and contrastive learning helps in learning representations that capture relevant patterns.

## [DreamQAS: Model-Based RL for Efficient Quantum Architecture Search](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29491) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DreamQAS introduces a model-based reinforcement learning framework for quantum architecture search (QAS) that learns a decision-useful world model, reducing reliance on expensive VQE evaluations. It achieves lower mean frozen-policy energy error on four of five molecular tasks and uses 1.6x to 2.0x fewer real VQE calls on four tasks, with 10.6x fewer on BeH2-8q. This work addresses a significant computational bottleneck in VQE-based quantum simulations, potentially accelerating the discovery of efficient quantum circuits. It demonstrates the value of model-based RL in quantum computing, offering a practical approach that could benefit quantum chemistry and materials science. The framework uses a recurrent randomized-prior ensemble to predict an oracle-free score relative to an empirical energy frontier, supporting multi-step imagined policy learning over explicit legal circuits. It incorporates ranking-based activation, uncertainty-aware pessimism and truncation, and selective real-VQE verification to form a reliability-controlled learning loop.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Variational quantum eigensolver (VQE) is a hybrid quantum-classical algorithm used to find ground state energies of molecules, but it requires many quantum circuit evaluations. Quantum architecture search (QAS) automates the design of quantum circuits, often using reinforcement learning (RL) to explore the circuit space. Traditional RL-QAS methods repeatedly run VQE, which is computationally expensive. Model-based RL learns a model of the environment to reduce real interactions, and DreamQAS applies this idea to QAS by learning only the expensive post-VQE feedback.

## [ARB Benchmark Reveals AI Detectors Fail on LLM-Rewritten Human Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29539) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ARB, a benchmark dataset with 1,800 human texts and four LLMs, creating matched variants to test detectors. Results show detectors like FastDetectGPT and Binoculars drop from ~90% recall on direct LLM text to ~15-30% on LLM-rewritten human text. This reveals a critical gap in AI-text detection evaluation, as real-world scenarios often involve LLM-rewritten human content. It suggests current detectors may be overestimated, impacting AI safety and content authenticity efforts. The benchmark uses four generators (Llama-3.2-3B, Qwen2.5-7B, Mistral-7B, Gemma-2-9B) and five detectors, evaluating at a strict 1% false-positive rate. Detectors retained high recall on LLM-rewritten LLM text (78-83%), but dropped sharply on human-to-LLM rewrites, indicating a specific vulnerability.

**Background**: AI-text detectors are tools that distinguish human-written text from LLM-generated text, often using statistical or neural methods. ## [MOT-SR: Multi-Objective Tool-Augmented Symbolic Regression Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29561) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MOT-SR is a new framework that integrates external analytical tools with large language models to perform symbolic regression, jointly optimizing accuracy, complexity, and generalization via a dynamic Pareto front. It outperforms existing methods on 40 standard tasks and shows improved trajectory-level accuracy in EMRI orbital modeling. This work addresses key limitations in LLM-based symbolic regression, such as lack of data analysis and single-objective evaluation, potentially improving scientific discovery efficiency. It also demonstrates applicability to long-horizon dynamics like gravitational-wave astronomy, which could benefit fields requiring interpretable and reliable models. MOT-SR uses two collaborative LLM modules: a Meta Strategy Generator that selects tools and synthesizes strategies based on Pareto-optimal equations, and an Equation Generator that produces new candidates. The closed-loop system continuously refines strategies and equations, and the framework is validated on EMRI orbital modeling, achieving the lowest trajectory-level integration error on held-out configurations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Symbolic regression (SR) searches for mathematical expressions that fit data, balancing accuracy and simplicity. Traditional methods often use genetic programming, while recent LLM-based approaches face challenges like premature convergence to local optima due to single-objective evaluation. Multi-objective optimization, which considers trade-offs among conflicting objectives, is a key concept here, and Pareto optimality is used to find a set of non-dominated solutions.

## [TraceViT: Grounded Trace Supervision Boosts Visual Abstract Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29586) ⭐️ 7.0/10

TraceViT, a looped visual reasoner, is trained with semantically monotonic transformation chains and soft trace alignment, achieving 67.8% pass@2 on ARC-AGI-1 and 24.3% on ARC-AGI-2. The method grounds each iteration with task references and object workspaces, improving intermediate refinement supervision. This work addresses a key limitation in training looped visual reasoners, where only the final output is constrained. By providing grounded trace supervision, it could improve performance on abstract reasoning benchmarks like ARC, bringing us closer to human-level AI. The method uses programmatic task implementations to generate intermediate grid states, and soft trace alignment enforces ordering without requiring exact length matching. Controlled ablations show that trace supervision is beneficial only when paired with grounding, highlighting the importance of the grounding mechanism.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: The Abstraction and Reasoning Corpus (ARC) is a benchmark introduced by François Chollet in 2019 to measure AI's ability to infer unseen transformations from few examples. Looped visual reasoners refine predictions iteratively, but conventional training only supervises the final output, leaving intermediate steps unconstrained. TraceViT introduces a method to supervise these intermediate steps using transformation chains derived from programmatic task implementations.

## [Human-Centered Validation of the EPC Score for XAI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29614) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces and validates the EPC score, a model-agnostic metric that extends the Explainability-Performance Coefficient (EPC) to quantify explanation quality by balancing feature selection sparsity and preserved model performance. The validation spans tabular, text, and image modalities and shows strong alignment with human explanations. This work addresses a critical open challenge in explainable AI: objectively evaluating explanation fidelity and aligning metrics with human understanding. By providing a validated, model-agnostic metric, it offers a practical tool for building trustworthy AI in high-risk domains. The EPC score is an extension of the previously introduced EPC metric, which quantifies the discriminative power of an explanation at a specific sparsity level. The validation includes comparisons against human lexical sentiment judgments and spatial visual annotations, demonstrating that higher EPC scores correlate with human-based explanations.

## [CENDRe: New Concept Extraction Method for Time-Series CNNs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29621) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CENDRe is a novel concept extraction method for CNNs that automatically discovers and localizes temporal and spectral concepts in time-series data. It overcomes three limitations of existing methods by operating in both time and frequency domains, automatically selecting the number of concepts, and producing better-aligned localizations. This method enhances interpretability of CNNs in critical domains like fault diagnosis, where understanding both temporal and spectral patterns is crucial. It provides more accurate and comprehensive concept extraction, potentially improving trust and reliability in AI systems. CENDRe uses two-stage clustering with silhouette-guided aggregation to automatically determine the number of concepts. It localizes concepts via gradients of a presence score, propagated through a differentiable invertible mapping like Fourier transform, yielding frequency-domain localizations. On synthetic benchmarks, it achieves representation correctness comparable to state-of-the-art and higher importance correctness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Concept extraction (CE) methods identify patterns in CNN latent space that drive predictions. Existing time-series CE methods only operate in the time domain, require a predefined number of concepts, and produce misaligned localizations. CENDRe addresses these by using frequency-domain analysis and automatic concept selection.

## [Socratic Tests: A New Theoretical Framework for AI-Driven Conversational Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29624) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the theoretical foundation for 'Socratic Tests,' an automated, multimodal conversational assessment that uses dynamic scaffolding and additive grading to map students' cognitive boundaries. It integrates Dynamic Assessment principles, multimodal workspaces, Bloom's Taxonomy, and the SOLO Taxonomy to formalize the quantification of the Zone of Proximal Development (ZPD). This framework addresses significant limitations of traditional static assessments and oral exams, such as deficit-based grading and construct-irrelevant variance. It has the potential to transform educational technology by enabling more accurate, personalized, and reliable assessments of student learning. The Socratic Test uses graduated scaffolding to quantify the Zone of Proximal Development (ZPD) and employs a non-compensatory, additive grading architecture that prioritizes mastery over penalty. It also emphasizes human-AI alignment to ensure unprecedented measurement reliability, though the paper is theoretical and lacks empirical validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Traditional static assessments use a subtractive, deficit-based grading model that penalizes errors and obscures diagnostic feedback. Dynamic Assessment, developed by Vygotsky, embeds interaction within a test-intervene-retest approach to assess learning potential. The SOLO Taxonomy (Structure of Observed Learning Outcomes) measures the complexity of student understanding, from prestructural to extended abstract levels. The Zone of Proximal Development (ZPD) refers to the difference between what a learner can do independently and what they can achieve with guidance.

## [LLM Control in Wargames: A Scoping Review](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17192) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper presents a scoping review of 223 AI-in-wargames papers, finding that only 20 (~9%) give language models open-ended control over both player actions and adjudication. It introduces a model-control profile framework to classify simulations. This review highlights a critical distinction in LLM-based simulations that is often overlooked, emphasizing that fidelity depends on whether models can act as reliable adjudicators. It provides a framework for researchers to better design and evaluate open-ended wargames and social simulations. The review covers papers retrieved through May 1, 2026, and classifies each simulation by whether the language model has open-ended control over actions, adjudication, or both. Only 20 studies give models both roles, suggesting a significant gap in current research.

**Background**: LLM-based social simulations use language models to generate transcripts that mimic human behavior, but the model may be responsible for different tasks: choosing actions, adjudicating outcomes, or both. In open-ended wargames, models are valued for handling unusual actions and ambiguous consequences, making the distinction between action control and adjudication crucial for faithful simulations.

## [DDC Framework Balances Budget and Quality in LLM Inference-Time Scaling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15100) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Dual-Dimensional Consistency (DDC), a unified framework that couples confidence-weighted Bayesian consensus with trend-aware stratified pruning to balance sampling budget and reasoning quality in LLM inference-time scaling. Evaluations across five benchmarks show over 10x reduction in token consumption while maintaining or exceeding baseline accuracy. This addresses a critical trade-off in LLM inference-time scaling, where current methods treat sampling width and depth as orthogonal, leading to inefficiencies like hallucination reinforcement or premature truncation. By unifying path quality and adaptive termination, DDC could enable more efficient and reliable LLM reasoning, benefiting applications that require high accuracy under compute constraints. The framework combines a Confidence-Weighted Bayesian protocol for consensus and a Trend-Aware Stratified Pruning mechanism for adaptive termination. It was evaluated across five benchmarks on various LLMs, achieving over 10x token reduction while maintaining or exceeding strong baseline accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Inference-time scaling (or test-time compute) refers to allocating additional computation to a model during inference to improve performance on challenging prompts. Current strategies often separate sampling width (number of parallel samples) and depth (length of reasoning chains), leading to inefficiencies. Bayesian consensus methods aggregate multiple samples by weighting votes based on reliability, while stratified pruning partitions data into strata for targeted pruning, as seen in other domains.

LabGuard introduces a language-to-execution safety suite that converts natural-language laboratory rules into executable specifications, deployed as runtime guards for embodied laboratory agents. It includes LabGuard-IR, LabGuard-Bench (812 annotations from 203 seed rules), and LabGuard-Grounder, achieving 79.4 task-scope F1 and reducing unsafe events from 39.5% to 23.8%. By grounding natural-language rules into machine-checkable constraints, LabGuard enhances safety and trustworthiness, potentially accelerating adoption of autonomous lab systems. LabGuard's runtime monitors integrate with the ACT controller in LabUtopia, keeping interventions below 0.5% while preserving task success. The framework generalizes to unseen laboratory-rule sources, demonstrating robustness across diverse protocols.

## [CodeRescue: Budget-Calibrated Recovery Routing for Coding Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.19338) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces CodeRescue, a budget-calibrated recovery routing method for coding agents that uses a supervised router with a Conformal Risk Control (CRC) layer to decide between cheap retries and expensive escalations after execution failures. The method provides marginal expected-cost control and, in the main GPT-5.4-nano/GPT-5.4 setting, achieves a solve rate exceeding always-escalate while using only 35% of its mean recovery cost. This work addresses a practical and timely problem in AI-assisted coding: how to balance cost and performance when agents recover from failures. By enabling budget-calibrated routing, it can significantly reduce deployment costs for coding agents without sacrificing solve rates, impacting both industry practitioners and researchers in ML systems and software engineering. The method trains a supervised router from execution rollouts and adds a CRC layer that selects a deployment-time cost penalty without retraining, providing marginal expected-cost control under exchangeability. Evaluated on held-out failures from five coding benchmarks, cheap recovery and escalation show complementary success patterns, and the calibrated frontier outperforms fixed actions, prompt-only routers, and a binary cascade baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Coding agents operate in executable environments where failed attempts produce actionable feedback. Existing cost-aware systems often use a cascade strategy: try a cheap model first, then escalate to a stronger, more expensive model. However, execution feedback can make further cheap-model recovery worthwhile, raising a budgeted deployment question. Conformal Risk Control (CRC) is a post-processing technique that provides statistical guarantees on risk, such as expected cost, without retraining.
