<a id="item-213"></a>
## [Auditing LLM Social Simulators via Reason-Mediated Behavioral Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24649) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel audit method for LLM social simulators that compares human rationale-derived reason states with LLM-simulated ones. Using a 94-person sunscreen concept test, it shows that human reasons improve behavior prediction, while LLM-simulated reasons are often plausible but fail to replicate the same reasoning. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLM social simulators by focusing on reasoning patterns rather than just outcomes. It provides a practical framework for auditing whether simulators align with human reasoning, which is essential for their reliable use in surveys and social science research. The method maps open-ended rationales into signed reason states Z, where positive signs support adoption and negative signs block it. The audit holds respondent descriptors, category context, and concept treatment fixed, and shows that LLM-simulated reasons often echo the concept board rather than recover the respondent's acceptance or rejection path.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: LLM social simulators are increasingly used as synthetic survey respondents, but most evaluations only check whether simulated outcomes match human outcomes. This paper argues that matching outcomes is necessary but insufficient, as a simulator could match the final answer while using incorrect reasoning. The proposed reason-mediated behavioral model provides an interpretable test of whether a simulator's stated reasons align with human evidence.

## [Sparse Geometric MPNNs Prove Expressive for Generic Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.02025) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proves that sparse geometric message-passing networks can separate generic non-isomorphic geometric graphs under connectivity and equivariance conditions, extending previous results from fully connected graphs. It introduces EGENNET, a simple architecture that achieves these theoretical guarantees. This result is significant because real-world applications in chemistry often use sparse graphs where each node only knows its nearest neighbors, not a fully connected graph. It provides theoretical guarantees for the expressive power of geometric MPNNs in practical settings, potentially guiding the design of more efficient and powerful models. The paper shows that with rotation equivariant features, generic separation is guaranteed as long as the underlying graph is connected. When only invariant intermediate features are allowed, generic separation is guaranteed for generically globally rigid graphs. The code is available at https://github.com/yonatansverdlov/E-GenNet.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Message-passing neural networks (MPNNs) are a class of graph neural networks that update node representations by aggregating messages from neighbors. Geometric MPNNs incorporate 3D positional information, which is crucial for tasks like molecular property prediction. Previous work showed that fully connected geometric MPNNs can separate generic non-isomorphic geometric graphs, but this paper extends the result to sparse graphs, which are more common in practice.

## [Dual-Force: Stable Offline Diversity Maximization via Van der Waals Objective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.04426) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Dual-Force is an offline RL algorithm that replaces mutual-information objectives with a Van der Waals force objective estimated from successor features, eliminating the skill discriminator. It also introduces Functional Reward Encoding (FRE) to stabilize training under non-stationary intrinsic rewards and enable zero-shot skill recall. This addresses instability issues in offline diversity maximization, a key challenge in reinforcement learning. By removing the skill discriminator and improving stability, Dual-Force could enable more robust skill discovery from demonstration data, benefiting applications like robotics and autonomous navigation. Dual-Force uses an off-policy estimator of the Van der Waals force objective computed from successor features, and conditions the value function and policy on a pre-trained Functional Reward Encoding (FRE). It was evaluated on two Solo12 simulation benchmarks (locomotion and obstacle navigation), showing improved robustness to adversarial obstacle variations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Offline diversity maximization aims to learn a set of distinct behaviors from a fixed dataset without additional environment interaction. Traditional methods often rely on mutual-information objectives that require a skill discriminator, which can be unstable under alternating Lagrangian optimization. The Van der Waals force objective, inspired by physics, provides a repulsive force between behaviors to encourage diversity, while Functional Reward Encoding (FRE) learns a latent representation of reward functions for zero-shot generalization.

## [DiRe: Force-Directed Dimensionality Reduction Preserving Global Structure and Homology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03156) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces DiRe, a force-directed dimensionality reduction framework that preserves global structure and homological features. It combines an initial embedding with graph-based layout optimization and evaluates results using local distortion, context preservation, and persistent homology measures. DiRe offers a complementary tradeoff to popular methods like UMAP and tSNE, which focus primarily on local structure. This provides a new tool for data visualization and analysis where large-scale geometry and topological features are important, potentially benefiting fields such as machine learning and topological data analysis. DiRe is designed to be practical on modern hardware, and its outputs can be quantified using Betti curves and persistence diagrams. The framework is positioned less as a purely local visualization heuristic and more as a method for embeddings whose large-scale geometry is measurable.

**Background**: Dimensionality reduction techniques like PCA, tSNE, and UMAP are widely used in machine learning and data visualization to reduce the number of features while preserving important structure. Force-directed layouts, originally from graph drawing, have been adopted in visualization libraries such as D3.js and Gephi. Homological stability and persistent homology are concepts from algebraic topology used to study the shape of data across scales, with Betti curves and persistence diagrams being common summaries.

## [StaQ: Finite-Memory Policy Mirror Descent for Discrete Actions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.13862) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces StaQ, a finite-memory approximation of Policy Mirror Descent (PMD) for discrete action spaces, which retains only the last M Q-functions. It provides theoretical proof that for sufficiently large M, the algorithm achieves convergence without policy update errors, and demonstrates empirically on MinAtar benchmarks that increasing M improves performance up to a threshold. This work bridges the gap between theoretical PMD and practical implementations by offering a tractable approximation that retains PMD's error-averaging property. It could influence the design of more stable and sample-efficient RL algorithms for discrete action tasks, benefiting both researchers and practitioners. The algorithm uses an efficient GPU implementation and is evaluated on medium-scale benchmarks like MinAtar. The theoretical analysis shows that an infinite sum of Q-functions is unnecessary; keeping the last M Q-functions is sufficient for convergence, with performance approaching exact PMD as M grows.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Policy Mirror Descent (PMD) is a family of RL algorithms that regularize policy updates with KL divergence to average out policy evaluation errors. However, exact PMD requires summing all past Q-functions, which is intractable in practice. Common approximations like natural policy gradient or actor-critic methods introduce errors in the policy update. StaQ addresses this by using a finite memory of Q-functions, providing a theoretically grounded and practical implementation.

## [Patch-Based 3D VAE Enables Scalable Super-Resolution of Turbulent Channel Flow](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.22082) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose a patch-based 3D variational autoencoder (3D-VAE) that reconstructs a local 16^3 high-resolution block from a larger coarse neighborhood, enabling scalable super-resolution of turbulent channel flow. The model achieves a mean absolute error of 0.055, outperforming tricubic (0.075) and Lanczos (0.076) interpolation, and reduces spectral error by about threefold. This work addresses a critical scalability bottleneck in 3D super-resolution for fluid dynamics, where model complexity typically grows with reconstructed volume. By making parameter count dependent only on patch size, it enables practical application to large-scale turbulent flow simulations, potentially accelerating scientific discovery in computational fluid dynamics. The model is trained on a single DNS snapshot of turbulent channel flow at Re_tau ≈ 1000 from the Johns Hopkins Turbulence Database and evaluated on a held-out snapshot. It is applied convolutionally with overlap averaging, and a conditional 3D-GAN trained on the same data failed to converge under Wasserstein training, reported as a negative result. Limitations include attenuation of smallest resolved scales, periodic artefacts from patch stride, and under-prediction of extreme velocity values.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Direct numerical simulation (DNS) resolves all spatio-temporal scales of wall-bounded turbulence but becomes prohibitively expensive at high Reynolds numbers. Super-resolution (SR) offers a practical alternative by reconstructing fine-scale structures from coarse fields, but most existing SR methods are limited to 2D data and extend poorly to 3D due to increasing model complexity. Variational autoencoders (VAEs) are generative models that learn a latent representation of data, and patch-based approaches allow processing of large volumes by dividing them into smaller blocks.

## [RePaCA: Reasoning LLMs Improve Static Patch Correctness Assessment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.22580) ⭐️ 7.0/10

RePaCA is a novel static APCA technique that uses reasoning large language models with Chain of Thought prompting to classify patches as correct or overfitting. It achieves state-of-the-art performance with 83.1% accuracy and an 84.8% F1-score on a Defects4J-derived benchmark. This work addresses the critical problem of overfitting patches in automated program repair, which can mislead developers and waste effort. By improving static patch correctness assessment with better accuracy and explainability, RePaCA could enhance the reliability of APR tools and benefit the software engineering community. The model is finetuned using Reinforcement Learning with the Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) algorithm to enhance reasoning for APCA. It also demonstrates superior generalization when trained on different datasets, outperforming leading techniques, and provides enhanced explainability through its reasoning process.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Automated Program Repair (APR) aims to automatically fix software bugs, but often generates overfitting patches that pass tests without truly fixing the underlying bug. Automated Patch Correctness Assessment (APCA) seeks to identify such patches, and static approaches rely only on the original and fixed code. Chain of Thought prompting elicits reasoning in large language models, which can be leveraged for this classification task.

## [LLM Corpus Reveals Social Biases Against Homelessness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.13187) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers released the first multi-domain corpus for detecting social biases against people experiencing homelessness (PEH), containing 1,698 gold-standard items and 48,389 GPT-4.1-labeled texts from Reddit, X, news, and city council transcripts across ten U.S. cities (2015-2025). They benchmarked six LLMs and found systematic miscalibration, such as over-tagging 'not in my backyard' (NIMBY) by 11.5 percentage points and under-detecting factual claims by 30.5 points. This work provides a valuable tool for municipalities to monitor stigma against homeless populations, addressing a critical social issue with practical applications. It also highlights limitations of LLMs in social bias detection, emphasizing the need for careful calibration and error analysis. The corpus uses a 16-category multi-label taxonomy and includes a stratified gold-standard set annotated by partner-trained raters. The study complements F1 scores with prevalence-gap audits and error analysis, showing that models often mistake housing vocabulary and question forms as opposition proxies, leading to NIMBY false positives on pro-service text.

## [Monotone and Separable Set Functions: Theory and Neural Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.23634) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Monotone and Separating (MAS) set functions that preserve the partial order on sets, provides dimension bounds for finite ground sets, proves impossibility for infinite ground sets, and proposes a weakly MAS neural model with Hölder stability. This work addresses a fundamental problem in set representation learning, offering theoretical guarantees that could improve models for set containment tasks. The impossibility result and the weakly MAS approximation provide a principled way to incorporate monotonicity as an inductive bias, potentially benefiting applications like recommendation systems and knowledge graph reasoning. The paper establishes lower and upper bounds on the vector dimension needed for MAS functions, depending on the cardinality of multisets and the ground set. For infinite ground sets, MAS functions do not exist, but the proposed weakly MAS model is Hölder continuous and can approximate all monotone set functions; experiments on set containment tasks show benefits over standard set models.

**Background**: Set-to-vector functions map sets to vectors, and preserving the subset relation (S⊆T iff F(S)≤F(T)) is desirable for tasks like set containment. Hölder continuity is a generalization of Lipschitz continuity, ensuring stability under small input changes. The paper's theoretical results guide the design of neural models that respect monotonicity.

## [Game-Theoretic Mechanism to Curb Dishonest LLM API Providers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.00847) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces the first formal economic model for a user-provider LLM ecosystem, where a user delegates T queries to multiple providers who may behave strategically. It proves the existence of an approximate incentive-compatible mechanism with an additive approximation ratio of O(T^{1-ε} log T) and a guaranteed quasi-linear second-best user utility, along with an impossibility result showing asymptotic optimality. This work addresses a critical trust issue in the growing LLM API market, where providers might substitute models or inflate tokens. The mechanism design offers a theoretical foundation for building reliable and fair pricing systems, potentially influencing how APIs are audited and regulated. The mechanism works for a continuous strategy space and any ε in (0, 1/2), achieving an additive approximation ratio of O(T^{1-ε} log T). Simulation experiments with real-world API settings demonstrate its effectiveness, and the impossibility result confirms no mechanism can asymptotically guarantee better user utility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are often accessed via APIs, where providers may engage in dishonest behaviors like model substitution (using a cheaper model while charging for a premium one) or token inflation (adding meaningless tokens to increase billing). Algorithmic game theory and mechanism design provide tools to design incentive-compatible systems where truthful behavior is optimal for all participants.

It decouples view-uncertainty modeling from the reconstructor, enabling precise uncertainty estimation without expensive online optimization, and achieves state-of-the-art reconstruction accuracy on scene-level and object-level benchmarks, especially in sparse-view settings. This work addresses a key limitation of existing active reconstruction methods that rely on hand-crafted geometric heuristics, which often lead to redundant observations. By leveraging feed-forward models and vision-language guidance, AREA3D can improve efficiency and quality in autonomous 3D reconstruction, with potential applications in robotics, AR/VR, and autonomous navigation. The framework decouples view-uncertainty modeling from the feed-forward reconstructor, allowing precise uncertainty estimation without online optimization. It also integrates a vision-language model to provide high-level semantic guidance, encouraging informative and diverse viewpoints beyond purely geometric cues. ## [GeoRA: Geometry-Aware Low-Rank Adaptation for RLVR](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.09361) ⭐️ 7.0/10

GeoRA introduces a geometry-aware low-rank adaptation method specifically designed for Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR), which extracts principal directions of the RL update subspace via SVD to initialize low-rank adapters while freezing residual components. Experiments on Qwen and Llama models (1.5B to 32B) show consistent improvements over strong low-rank baselines in mathematics, medicine, and coding tasks. This work addresses a critical gap in parameter-efficient fine-tuning for RLVR, which has distinct optimization dynamics compared to SFT. By preserving pre-trained geometric structures and enabling efficient dense computation, GeoRA could enhance the training of large-scale reasoning models, making them more efficient and less prone to forgetting. GeoRA leverages the anisotropic and compressible structure of the RL update subspace, using SVD to identify principal directions for adapter initialization. The method freezes residual components as a structural anchor, which preserves the pre-trained structure and allows dense computation, overcoming efficiency bottlenecks of sparse fine-tuning on modern hardware.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement Learning with Verifiable Rewards (RLVR) is a paradigm where models receive rewards based on objective, automatically verifiable criteria, such as correct answers or passing unit tests. Low-rank adaptation methods like LoRA and PiSSA are commonly used for parameter-efficient fine-tuning, but they are primarily designed for supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and may not suit RLVR's unique optimization dynamics. GeoRA builds on these concepts by tailoring low-rank adaptation to the geometric properties of RLVR updates.

## [KRPO: Label-Free Prompt Optimization for Open-Domain Triplet Extraction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.15037) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces KRPO, a label-free prompt optimization framework that adapts LLMs to open-domain relational triplet extraction by restoring extracted triplets into textual statements and using semantic consistency with source inputs as intrinsic feedback. It also proposes a Memory-augmented Relation Canonicalizer to align free-form relations with a dynamically updated schema memory. This work addresses a key challenge in open-domain relational triplet extraction: adapting LLMs to varying contexts without labeled data. By enabling label-free adaptation and improving relation consistency, KRPO could enhance knowledge graph construction and reduce reliance on manual prompt engineering, benefiting NLP practitioners and researchers. KRPO achieves the best average F1 score across three ORTE benchmarks with multiple LLM backbones. The framework is label-free, using intrinsic feedback derived from semantic consistency, and includes a Memory-augmented Relation Canonicalizer to address non-canonical relation surface forms.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Open-domain Relational Triplet Extraction (ORTE) aims to extract structured knowledge without predefined relation schemas, often using LLMs with in-context learning. Traditional methods rely on fixed manual prompts, which fail to adapt to varying linguistic expressions, leading to unsupported triplets and inconsistent relation forms. Label-free prompt optimization methods, such as Prompt Duel Optimizer, use LLM judges to provide feedback without ground-truth labels, but may suffer from overfitting or judge bias. KRPO builds on these ideas by using knowledge restoration to generate intrinsic feedback.

## [HOLO: Speeding Up Short-Text Generation via Holographic Characteristic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.22546) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper identifies a 'Holographic Characteristic' in LLMs, where target-side keywords are captured early in generation, and introduces HOLO, a plugin that leverages this trait to speed up short-text generation. This work offers a novel approach to improving LLM inference efficiency without requiring a separate draft model, potentially reducing latency and cost for short-text generation tasks. It highlights a previously underexplored generation trait that could inspire further optimization strategies. HOLO extracts target-side keywords from the probability distributions of the first two generation steps, then uses the lexically constrained text generation method POINTER to complete the sentence in parallel. Experiments across various LLM architectures and scales show comparable performance to baselines in short-text generation.

## [Curvature-Aware MDL Framework for Layer-Adaptive LLM Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00910) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a curvature-aware, MDL-inspired framework that converts layer sensitivity estimates into optimal capacity allocation and pruning decisions under hardware budgets, using convex programs solvable in O(K log(1/ε)) time. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM compression by providing a principled, budget-feasible optimization procedure instead of heuristic score-to-decision mappings, potentially improving efficiency and performance of deployed models. The framework defines a layer gain ζ_k² = g_kᵀ H̃_kk⁻¹ g_k, which is twice the maximal decrease from a regularized layer-restricted quadratic model. Experiments on Mistral-7B and Gemma-7B show allocation gains in some settings and competitive but mixed pruning performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) have non-uniform layer-wise capacity, with some layers contributing more to loss reduction. Layer pruning and capacity allocation are common compression techniques, but existing methods often rely on heuristics. The Minimum Description Length (MDL) principle formalizes Occam's Razor, favoring simpler models that compress data well. Curvature-aware optimization uses second-order information to guide parameter updates, which can be more effective than gradient-only methods.

This paper introduces DBench-Bio, a dynamic and fully automated benchmark designed to evaluate LLMs' ability to discover new biological knowledge. The benchmark is instantiated with a pipeline that uses LLMs to synthesize scientific hypothesis questions and discovery answers from authoritative paper abstracts, followed by a QA filter for quality control. This paper proposes a novel framework that uses functionally imperfect LLM-generated RTL as training data for netlist representation learning, exploiting structural patterns that remain indicative of intended functionality. The approach forms an end-to-end pipeline from automated code generation to downstream tasks like sub-circuit boundary identification and component classification. This work addresses the data scarcity bottleneck in circuit representation learning by providing a cost-effective way to generate large-scale training data without relying on expensive, IP-protected real designs. It could significantly advance hardware design automation by enabling models to generalize better to real-world netlists, potentially matching or surpassing methods trained on scarce high-quality data. The framework is evaluated on circuit functional understanding tasks, including sub-circuit boundary identification and component classification, across benchmarks of increasing scales, extending from operator-level to IP-level tasks. Models trained on the noisy synthetic corpus generalize well to real-world netlists, demonstrating the effectiveness of the approach.

## [CAP-TTA: Test-Time Adaptation for OOD Debiasing in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13683) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces CAP-TTA, a test-time adaptation framework that applies context-aware LoRA updates only when a bias-risk score exceeds a threshold, using an offline precomputed diagonal preconditioner for fast and stable optimization. It effectively reduces toxicity/bias on out-of-distribution prompts with lower latency than standard optimizers like AdamW or SGD. This work addresses a critical limitation of debiased LLMs: their failure on unfamiliar, high-bias prompts. By enabling real-time correction with low latency and preventing catastrophic forgetting, CAP-TTA offers a practical solution for deploying safer LLMs in dynamic environments, potentially improving trust and reliability in real-world applications. CAP-TTA uses a bias-risk score to trigger context-aware LoRA updates, and an offline precomputed diagonal preconditioner ensures fast and stable optimization. It demonstrates reduced toxicity/bias with significantly lower latency than AdamW or SGD, while also improving narrative fluency over state-of-the-art baselines without compromising debiasing performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time adaptation (TTA) allows models to adapt to new data distributions during inference without retraining. LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that injects trainable low-rank matrices into frozen models. Out-of-distribution (OOD) detection identifies when input data deviates from the training distribution, which is crucial for triggering adaptation mechanisms.

## [OPERA: Efficient Data Pruning for Dense Retrieval Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17205) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OPERA introduces a data pruning framework for dense retrievers, featuring static pruning (SP) and a two-stage dynamic pruning (DP) strategy. DP achieves the best performance, improving NDCG@10 by +1.0 points and Recall@20 by +0.4 points over standard fine-tuning, while reducing training time by over 50%. This work addresses the quality-coverage tradeoff in data pruning for dense retrieval, offering a practical method to improve both ranking and retrieval performance while cutting training costs. It is architecture-agnostic, as demonstrated on Qwen3-Embedding, making it valuable for efficient model adaptation across domains. The DP strategy adaptively modulates sampling probabilities at both query and document levels throughout training, maintaining access to the full training set. Evaluations span eight datasets across six domains, and DP achieves an average rank of 1.38 across all methods.

NDCG and Recall are standard metrics for evaluating ranking quality and retrieval coverage in information retrieval.

This paper introduces Predict-then-Diffuse, a model-agnostic framework that estimates the optimal response length before generation in diffusion-based LLMs, enabling compute-budgeted inference. It includes an Adaptive Response Length Predictor (AdaRLP) and a data-driven safety mechanism to prevent truncation. Diffusion LLMs require a fixed response length, causing waste or truncation; this framework addresses that limitation, potentially improving efficiency and reducing latency. It could make diffusion LLMs more practical for real-world applications where compute budgets vary. The framework uses AdaRLP to predict the optimal length and applies a small increase to avoid underestimation, avoiding re-computation. Experiments show significant FLOP reduction compared to default D-LLM inference, with robustness to skewed data distributions.

Compute-budgeted inference aims to allocate compute resources adaptively based on the query's complexity.

## [SNN-Based Detector Exploits Temporal Smoothness Gaps to Spot AI Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.05895) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MAST, a detector using Spiking Neural Networks (SNNs) to identify AI-generated videos by exploiting temporal smoothness gaps in pixel-level residuals and semantic feature trajectories. On the GenVideo benchmark, MAST achieves 93.14% mean accuracy across 10 unseen generators under strict cross-generator evaluation. This work demonstrates the practical applicability of SNNs to AI-generated video detection, a timely problem as synthetic videos become more photorealistic. By achieving competitive performance with ANN-based detectors while being event-driven and sparse, it could inspire more energy-efficient and biologically plausible detection methods. MAST processes multi-channel temporal residuals with a spike-driven temporal branch alongside a frozen semantic encoder for cross-generator generalization. The paper identifies that fake clips elicit firing predominantly at object and motion boundaries in SNNs, unlike real clips, indicating localized temporal artifacts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Spiking neural networks (SNNs) are artificial neural networks that mimic biological neural networks by using discrete spikes as the main information carrier, incorporating time into their operation. Unlike traditional ANNs, SNNs transmit information only when a neuron's membrane potential reaches a threshold, making them event-driven and sparsely activated. This property aligns well with the sparse, asynchronous nature of temporal artifacts in AI-generated videos, which is why the authors chose SNNs for detection.

## [Quantization Breaks Recourse: New Metrics and Mitigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17160) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces counterfactual sensitivity under quantization, proposing two metrics—Validity Drop (VD) and Counterfactual Recourse Gap (CRG)—to measure how quantization affects algorithmic recourse, and a method called Counterfactual-Faithful Quantization (CFQ) to mitigate the failure mode. This work addresses a critical gap in model deployment: quantization can preserve accuracy while degrading recourse stability, which is vital for fairness and user trust in decision systems. It provides metrics and a solution that can be integrated into standard quantization pipelines, benefiting practitioners in high-stakes domains. CFQ is compatible with standard LSQ/PACT-style quantizers and mixed-precision policies, and can also be instantiated as a training-free calibration procedure for post-training quantization. Experiments on Adult, German Credit, and COMPAS show that CFQ reduces VD/CRG significantly; for example, on Adult, it reduces VD/CRG from 0.121/0.162 to 0.061/0.071 at matched accuracy and bit budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Model quantization reduces memory and latency by lowering numerical precision, but it is typically evaluated only by predictive accuracy. Algorithmic recourse provides actionable counterfactual explanations to users, and its stability under compression is often overlooked. This paper bridges these areas, highlighting a deployment mismatch and offering a solution.

## [AI4BayesCode: LLM-Driven Generation of Validated Modular MCMC Samplers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18476) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AI4BayesCode is a new system that uses large language models to translate natural-language descriptions of Bayesian models into runnable, validated MCMC samplers. It introduces a modular design and a recursively stateful coding paradigm to improve reliability and composability. This addresses a major bottleneck in Bayesian workflows, where coding and computation are often time-consuming and error-prone. By automating sampler generation, it could make advanced Bayesian methods more accessible to researchers and practitioners, potentially accelerating progress in fields that rely on Bayesian inference. The system performs pre-generation validation of model specifications and post-generation validation of generated code to ensure correctness. It also includes a benchmark suite for evaluating sampler generation, and the software is available at ai4bayescode.com.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC) is a widely used technique for sampling from complex probability distributions, such as posterior distributions in Bayesian inference. Traditional probabilistic programming systems often have limited model support and extensibility, making it difficult to implement custom samplers. AI4BayesCode aims to overcome these limitations by using LLMs to automate the coding process, breaking models into modular blocks that map to built-in sampling components.

## [LoRA Fine-Tuning of Qwen2.5-3B for Telecom Support with Energy Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05176) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper systematically evaluates 16 LoRA configurations for fine-tuning Qwen2.5-3B on telecom customer support, using a synthetic dataset of ~30,000 examples generated via Gemini 2.0 Flash. It also incorporates energy consumption analysis and LLM-as-a-judge evaluation with GPT-5.2 and Claude 4.5 Sonnet. This work provides practical insights into efficient domain adaptation of small language models (SLMs) under resource constraints, highlighting the limitations of validation loss as a selection metric. The energy-performance trade-off analysis is valuable for sustainable AI deployment in industry settings. The best validation loss (0.5024) ranked only 6th-7th in qualitative evaluation, while the worst loss (0.6807) ranked first according to both judges. The study introduces a combinatorial synthetic data generation method based on a glossary of 52 industry-specific terms, producing 1,560 distinct problem scenarios.

**Background**: Parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) methods like Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) allow adapting large pre-trained models by updating only a small fraction of parameters, reducing computational cost. Qwen2.5-3B is a 3-billion-parameter open-source language model from Alibaba, suitable for domain-specific tasks. This study applies these techniques to telecom customer support, addressing data sovereignty and regulatory constraints.

## [MSFAN: Multi-Scale Attention Network for Polymer Classification via THz Spectroscopy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.06554) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces MSFAN, a novel deep learning architecture that integrates feature gating, multi-scale parallel convolutions, cross-feature attention, and attention pooling to classify 12 polymer types using terahertz spectroscopy, achieving 85.2% accuracy. This work addresses a critical challenge in plastic recycling by providing a scalable, non-destructive, and interpretable method for polymer identification, which could improve sorting efficiency and material quality in recycling facilities. It also demonstrates the potential of combining THz spectroscopy with advanced deep learning techniques for industrial applications. MSFAN outperforms state-of-the-art models, achieving 85.2% classification accuracy across pure polymers, multilayer films, commercial blends, and biopolymers. The architecture includes spectral recalibration via feature gating, multi-scale feature extraction, global spectral contextualization, and a classification head with sparse optimization.

However, conventional sorting methods and simple spectroscopic analysis often fail to robustly distinguish similar polymers. Deep learning models, especially those with attention mechanisms, can automatically learn complex patterns in spectral data, improving classification performance.

## [Decidable Criterion for Creative Integration via Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.13977) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a decidable criterion for creative integration (CI), defined as a strict reduction in description length (C = L_pre/L_post > 1) that resolves a conflict, validated by four falsifiable tests rather than human agreement. This provides an operational, testable definition of creative integration, which could significantly impact how AI creativity is evaluated and understood, moving beyond subjective human judgment. The criterion uses four binary, conjunctive gates to make the judgment decidable, and includes a taxonomy of pseudo-integration to reject look-alikes. It is backed by a curated multi-domain corpus and passes all four falsifiable tests: independent computational check, discrimination against hard negatives, out-of-sample prediction, and description-language robustness.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: The paper builds on the idea that creativity and intelligence can be understood as compression. Falsifiability, as a scientific standard, means a statement can be contradicted by empirical observation. This work applies that principle to creativity, offering a way to distinguish genuine integration from mere re-description.

## [PauseRec: Implicit Reasoning Boosts LLM Generative Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.14142) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper systematically analyzes explicit reasoning pipelines in LLM-based generative recommendation, identifying three key limitations, and proposes PauseRec, a lightweight implicit reasoning paradigm that outperforms explicit CoT methods by up to 6.22% while reducing training cost by up to 65% GPU hours and speeding up inference by up to 71.3%. This work challenges the prevailing assumption that explicit reasoning is necessary for LLM-based generative recommendation, offering a more efficient and effective alternative. It could significantly reduce the computational burden of deploying such systems and improve their practical viability in real-world applications. The three limitations identified are weakened world-knowledge verbalization, misalignment between SID and natural-language token embedding spaces, and sensitivity to rationale quality. PauseRec avoids costly reasoning trace acquisition and reasoning alignment training, making it exceptionally practical.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: LLM-based generative recommendation represents items using Semantic IDs (SIDs), which are unseen tokens that disrupt the LLM's natural-language reasoning interface. Existing approaches use multi-stage pipelines to ground SIDs and elicit explicit rationales, but these are expensive and offer limited insight into when and why each stage is necessary. Implicit reasoning, in contrast, is faster and more opaque, akin to intuition, and has been explored in other AI contexts.

## [SqLinear: Balanced Square Partitioning Enables Scalable Linear Traffic Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21072) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SqLinear introduces a geometry-adaptive Square Partition algorithm that divides traffic sensors into balanced, non-overlapping, compact regions, and a Hierarchical Linear Interaction (HLI) module that replaces attention with linear interactions. Experiments on four large-scale datasets show it reduces MAE by 2.30% on average and up to 6.78% under extreme scalability, while cutting training runtime by 13.27%–30.84%. This work addresses a critical scalability bottleneck in deploying neural traffic forecasting models to real-world urban networks with thousands of sensors. By achieving linear complexity and improved accuracy, SqLinear could enable more efficient and practical intelligent transportation systems, influencing future designs in spatial-temporal modeling. Square Partition provides provable guarantees on partition utilization and split balance, unlike handcrafted heuristics. HLI propagates global inter-region dependencies and refines them at the node level with linear computational complexity, avoiding the costly attention mechanisms of Transformers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Traffic forecasting is essential for intelligent transportation systems, but mainstream neural methods like Transformers suffer from poor scalability when applied to large sensor networks. Prior attempts used spatial database partitioning, but they relied on geometric heuristics that produced irregular or imbalanced partitions, causing boundary fragmentation and padding overhead. SqLinear's balanced square partitioning and linear interaction offer a theoretically grounded alternative.

## [DRIFT: A New Framework for LLM Self-Improvement via Difficulty Routing and Rhythm Gating](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.30345) ⭐️ 7.0/10

DRIFT is an online self-evolution policy optimization framework that introduces Difficulty Routing and Rhythm Gating to regulate LLM self-improvement. It outperforms GRPO and SDPO on five benchmarks, achieving an average score of 79.5% and setting a new state-of-the-art on ToolUse with 79.2% accuracy. This work addresses a critical challenge in LLM self-improvement by providing explicit mechanisms to track problem-level learning progress and adapt optimization strategies, potentially leading to more stable and efficient autonomous learning. It could benefit the AI community by improving performance on complex reasoning tasks without external supervision. DRIFT uses Difficulty Routing to dynamically allocate self-distillation and reinforcement learning signals at the problem level, and Rhythm Gating to refine policy updates at the token level. It also incorporates a success buffer and a two-stage curriculum learning strategy to preserve high-quality historical experience and guide the model from reliable behavior acquisition to stable policy evolution.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) often struggle to improve themselves without external expert supervision, as existing self-distillation and reinforcement learning methods may over-optimize easy problems, receive weak supervision from hard problems, and fail to explore borderline cases. Difficulty routing is a technique that matches model capability to task difficulty, often used in LLM routing to reduce costs, while rhythm gating is a novel concept that controls the timing of policy updates at the token level. These mechanisms help address the limitations of previous approaches.

## [AuEmoChat: Authentic Emotion Understanding and Rendering for Conversational Speech Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15755) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AuEmoChat introduces a novel framework for conversational speech synthesis that uses AuEmoCodec to learn discrete authentic emotion tokens via finite scalar quantization, and AuEmoToMe to merge redundant multimodal tokens while preserving emotion-relevant context. The framework outperforms state-of-the-art baselines on the NCSSD-EmCap dataset, generating more expressive and authentic emotional speech. This work addresses a key limitation in conversational speech synthesis: the reliance on predefined emotion categories (e.g., seven basic emotions) which fail to capture the nuance of human emotion. By learning an authentic emotion token space and improving context understanding, AuEmoChat could enable more natural and emotionally expressive human-computer interactions, benefiting applications like virtual assistants, gaming, and accessibility tools. AuEmoCodec uses finite scalar quantization (FSQ) to create a discrete emotion token space from large-scale emotional speech, avoiding the limitations of categorical labels. AuEmoToMe is an emotion-guided token merging algorithm that reduces redundancy in multimodal dialogue history while preserving emotion-relevant context, and the framework integrates these into an autoregressive text-speech model with Authentic Emotion Flow Matching for speech rendering.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Conversational speech synthesis (CSS) aims to generate speech that matches the emotional tone and context of a user-agent dialogue. Traditional methods often rely on a fixed set of emotion labels, which oversimplify human emotions, and they struggle with redundant information in multi-turn dialogue history. Token merging (ToMe) is a technique originally developed for vision transformers to speed up inference by combining similar tokens, and finite scalar quantization (FSQ) is a simplified vector quantization method that discretizes continuous representations into a finite set of scalar levels.

EduPanel is a novel three-agent LLM judge that provides learner-conditioned, rubric-grounded evaluations of teaching videos, achieving reliability comparable to a median human expert and improving expert scoring accuracy (MAE from 0.87 to 0.73) while maintaining human oversight. This work addresses the growing need for scalable evaluation of teaching videos, offering a multi-agent approach that can assist human experts rather than replace them. It has potential implications for educational technology and AI evaluation, particularly in human-AI collaboration settings. EduPanel decomposes evaluation across specialized agents to produce interpretable assessments for different aspects of teaching quality. In expert studies, experts could detect unreliable outputs with an AUC of 0.77, indicating that human oversight remains effective.

## [ElasticTTT: Preventing Prior Collapse in Test-Time Video Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.21529) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ElasticTTT introduces a novel framework to prevent prior collapse during test-time tuning for video editing, proposing three techniques: Target Distribution Regularization, Contrastive CFG, and Asynchronous Noise Schedule. The paper demonstrates state-of-the-art performance on one-shot video editing with theoretical support. This work addresses a critical mismatch in test-time tuning for generative models, which can lead to prior collapse and degraded editing quality. By preserving the generative prior, ElasticTTT could improve the reliability and applicability of diffusion-based video editing in real-world scenarios. The framework includes Target Distribution Regularization to avoid sharp memorization minima, Contrastive CFG to steer inference away from source biases, and Asynchronous Noise Schedule to preserve unedited regions. Extensive evaluations and theoretical analysis support the effectiveness of ElasticTTT.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Test-time tuning (TTT) adapts pretrained diffusion models for specific tasks like video editing, but standard TTT optimizes a single point, conflicting with the distribution-mapping nature of generative models. This mismatch can cause prior collapse, where the model ignores text conditions and latents, collapsing outputs to the source video or entangling features. Prior collapse is related to model collapse, a degradation phenomenon in generative models trained on their own outputs, but here it occurs during adaptation rather than training.

## [Compositional Runtime Verification Framework for LLM-Assisted ISR Swarms](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23532) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes a three-tier (platform/squad/mission) compositional runtime verification framework that decomposes mission policies into per-agent and cross-agent aspects, aggregates verdicts over a verification-aware messaging fabric, and fuses them with an evidence-aware two-axis algebra to detect mission-level violations that per-platform guardrails miss. This addresses a critical gap in assuring LLM-assisted autonomous swarms, where individually compliant actions can compose into mission-level violations. It is timely for AI safety and autonomous systems, offering a way to detect cross-agent failures with provenance, which is essential for deploying such swarms in contested environments. The framework uses a verification-aware messaging fabric that makes evidence loss and silence observable, downgrading unsupported negative verdicts to an explicit 'unknown' rather than reporting mission-wide all-clears. In a simulated ISR mission, an indirect prompt injection causing LLM planners to split a prohibited task across four platforms was detected compositionally with full provenance, while a best-effort central monitor emitted silent false all-clears.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Runtime verification is a technique that analyzes execution traces of a running system to detect violations of specified properties, often expressed in temporal logics or automata. LLM-assisted autonomous robots are increasingly proposed for cooperative ISR, but assurance failures can arise across the swarm rather than within a single platform, which per-platform guardrails cannot catch. This paper introduces a compositional approach to address that gap.

## [LEX-EC: Black-Box Audit Framework for LLM Personality Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24435) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces LEX-EC, a reusable black-box audit framework that combines prevalence and agreement diagnostics with controlled lexical ablation to evaluate LLM-based personality classification across text genres. It reveals that trait associations vary with lexical evidence, with free-form essays showing broad but weak signal, and single Facebook statuses yielding little stable evidence. This framework addresses the interpretability gap in LLM-based personality classification, offering a method to audit black-box models without internal access. It provides empirical insights that could guide more reliable use of LLMs in psychological assessment and social media analysis. LEX-EC uses lexical ablation to distinguish marginal-distribution effects from trait-associated signal, and evaluates classification prevalence, item-level association, chance-corrected agreement, persistence under lexical restriction, and prompt sensitivity. Masking topical and demographic content weakened some associations while leaving others detectable from function words, affective terms, and cognitive-style vocabulary.

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) can assign personality labels from text, but their interpretability remains a challenge. Lexical ablation is a technique that systematically removes or masks certain words or categories to observe their impact on model outputs, helping to identify which lexical features drive predictions. This framework applies such methods in a black-box setting, where only inputs and outputs are accessible, to audit personality classification.

The paper introduces PALATE, a scalable benchmark for evaluating role-playing agents (RPAs) using person-aligned LLM-simulated users. It trains five per-user simulators that engage in free-form multi-turn conversations with candidate RPAs, and uses personalized rubrics to measure user satisfaction, showing higher agreement with human judgments than a general rubric. PALATE includes a pool of 300 character profiles and evaluates 16 candidate RPAs. It separately characterizes generic turn quality, long-horizon session capability, and per-user experience, producing evaluations for specific user-RPA pairs rather than a single user-independent ranking.

## [EOMR: Joint Feature Subset and Subspace Learning for Nonlinear Regression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28080) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Entropy-Optimal Manifold Regression (EOMR), an extension of EOMC that simultaneously learns relevant feature subsets and subspaces in nonstationary and nonlinear regression problems. EOMR achieves linear scaling in iteration and memory complexity and outperforms state-of-the-art ML and AI tools on chaotic and plasma dynamics benchmarks. EOMR addresses a critical gap in regression methods by jointly optimizing feature selection and subspace learning, which is particularly valuable for high-dimensional, nonstationary systems. Its linear scaling and superior performance on challenging benchmarks suggest it could become a powerful tool for scientific and engineering applications where traditional methods fail. EOMR was validated on Lorenz-96 systems in strongly and very-strongly chaotic regimes (F=8 and F=12) and on Hasegawa-Wakatani model data from tokamak plasma edge. For the Hasegawa-Wakatani example, EOMR distilled a simple 8-parameter linear autoregressive model for the leading EOF dynamics, while gradient-boosted random forests, deep neural networks, and TabPFN v.03 produced orders of magnitude worse errors.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: EOMC (Entropy-Optimal Manifold Clustering) is a recently proposed method that extends PCA clustering with metricization and entropy regularization to handle nonstationary and nonlinear data. The Lorenz-96 model is a classic chaotic dynamical system used to test prediction methods, while the Hasegawa-Wakatani model describes plasma turbulence in magnetic confinement fusion devices. These benchmarks are notoriously difficult for standard ML tools due to their chaotic and high-dimensional nature.

## [Ontology-Guided Extraction Layer Builds Validated Knowledge Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28662) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper presents a production extraction layer that converts heterogeneous documents into a validated knowledge graph using a locally hosted Qwen3.5-9B model and ontology-guided retrieval, reducing catalog overhead by about 94% and improving search recall from roughly 70% to 95%. This work addresses critical challenges in knowledge graph construction, such as entity and relationship deduplication, which are essential for accurate data integration. The system's practical design and significant improvements in recall and defect correction could influence future AI-driven data management systems. The extraction pipeline includes five refinement stages: deterministic cleaning, merging across chunks, a second pass for relationships, six deduplication algorithms requiring no model inference, and an embedding resolution subsystem with a conflict guard. The system processes documents from Kafka, handling PDF, spreadsheet, Office, and image formats, and uses a locally hosted Qwen3.5-9B model tuned on the ontology.

**Background**: Knowledge graphs organize information as entities and relationships, but extracting them from unstructured documents often leads to inconsistencies and duplicates. Ontology-guided extraction uses a formal ontology to define entity types and relationships, improving consistency. Deduplication and entity resolution are crucial to avoid fragmented and inaccurate graphs. The Qwen3.5-9B model is a recent open-source LLM that can run locally, enabling privacy-preserving and cost-effective processing.

## [Evidence-Grounded Constraint Checking in Construction Documents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29058) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces an evidence-grounded pipeline for constraint checking in construction documents, and evaluates a PDF evidence allocator on 160 tasks from 29 projects. In a repeated test, reallocating image evidence from page overviews to overlapping tiles improved decision accuracy by 10.6 percentage points, but this effect did not persist in a broader evaluation. This work addresses a niche but critical domain—professional document review—and highlights a resolution-breadth trade-off in evidence allocation for RAG systems. The findings could inform the design of rule-aware evidence routing in document AI, potentially improving accuracy in similar constraint-checking tasks. The pipeline normalizes extracted facts, executes four-state rules deterministically, retains source spans, and escalates unresolved cases. The repeated test used a four-image budget, reallocating from page overviews to one overview and three overlapping tiles, yielding a 10.6-point improvement (95% CI: 4.3 to 18.0; p=0.031), but the broader block showed a -4.1-point change (95% CI: -10.2 to 1.9; p=0.209). Exact finding-set recovery remains low, false passes are common, and repeated-run agreement is poorly calibrated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Professional-document review is a constraint-checking problem where decisions depend on relations among text, geometry, pages, and revisions. The paper compares page-RAG and region-RAG, which differ only in visual evidence allocation, not in prompts or decision logic. This work is relevant to retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems, where evidence allocation can impact accuracy.

## [FDD-ON: A New Ontology for VAV HVAC Fault Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29657) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces FDD-ON, a modular and extensible ontology that formally represents VAV HVAC system components, fault types, symptom statuses, fault impacts, and associated attributes. It integrates HVAC FDD semantics and provides comprehensive fault, symptom, and impact libraries to support interoperable FDD applications. FDD-ON addresses the lack of data interoperability in the FDD domain, which has hindered the implementation of digital twin-enabled FDD frameworks and AI-driven maintenance systems. By providing a machine-interpretable semantic foundation, it can facilitate scalable, transparent, and interoperable FDD solutions across various building applications. FDD-ON is evaluated using publicly available VAV HVAC system datasets and demonstrated through FDD development applications. It defines explicit contributing cause-fault-symptom-impact relations, enabling querying of diagnostic knowledge and mapping of heterogeneous FDD outputs.

## [Scaffolding Critical GenAI Engagement in Ethnic Minority Education](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28630) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This design-based research study with 78 ethnic minority preparatory students in China demonstrates that pedagogical scaffolding, including a human-in-the-loop workflow and teacher modeling, can shift students from passive GenAI consumption to critical co-creation, as evidenced by epistemic network analysis and increased prompt self-efficacy. This research addresses the critical risk of cognitive laziness in GenAI use among marginalized student groups, offering a replicable pedagogical model that promotes epistemic agency and educational equity. It underscores that technical training alone is insufficient, providing a framework for educators to design targeted interventions for human-AI collaboration. The study employed epistemic network analysis to analyze collaborative discourse, thematic analysis for student reflections, and paired-samples t-tests for prompt self-efficacy changes. Results revealed a 'strategic repurposing' phenomenon where students initially used strategy talk for efficient copying but later realigned it to scaffold critical evaluation and peer co-construction.

**Background**: Epistemic Network Analysis (ENA) is a mathematical technique used to quantify connections among coded data, often applied in learning sciences to model knowledge structures. Design-based research (DBR) is an iterative methodology in education that develops and tests interventions in real-world settings. A human-in-the-loop (HITL) workflow integrates human oversight into automated processes, which is crucial for ensuring quality and ethical use of AI in education.

## [HenTwin: Multimodal Digital Twin Framework for Poultry Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28652) ⭐️ 6.0/10

HenTwin introduces a five-layer IoT architecture and a four-dimensional biological state vector to monitor laying hens from hatch to 25 weeks, using longitudinal multimodal data from 150 Lohmann LSL-Lite hens. The framework estimates a discrete-time state transition model and detects coordinated developmental transitions at Weeks 12-14. This work addresses the fragmentation in poultry monitoring by providing a formal, state-aware digital twin approach, which could improve early-life health management and precision livestock farming. The framework's cross-room validation and two-tier deployment architecture suggest practical scalability for broader agricultural applications. The four-dimensional state vector includes body surface temperature, acoustic energy entropy, band energy ratio, and optical-flow-based motion, with temperature-humidity index as an exogenous input. Perturbation analysis shows a sustained +2.0 THI increase leads to a stable acoustic entropy elevation of 0.54 nats, about one-quarter of the 1.87-nat developmental decline.

In agriculture, IoT architectures collect multimodal data from sensors to model animal health and behavior. The paper introduces SEDR-Seq2P, a lightweight Seq2Point extension with dilated residual blocks and squeeze-and-excitation attention, achieving about 7% MAE reduction and about 1% R² improvement over Seq2Point on the IMDELD dataset for multi-task industrial NILM. This work addresses the challenge of industrial NILM, where residential-tuned models fail due to noise and concurrent machine operation. SEDR-Seq2P reduces inference latency by approximately 58% compared to WaveNet while improving match rate by about 0.8%. ## [Metasignal: Python Package for Metacognitive Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29093) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Metasignal is a newly released open-source Python package that implements 17 metacognitive measures for signal detection theory (SDT) and metacognitive analysis in binary discrimination tasks. It provides a unified platform with a single function to compute all measures from trial-level data, along with CLI, group summaries, bootstrap confidence intervals, permutation tests, and optional hierarchical Bayesian models. This package addresses the fragmentation in metacognition research by unifying multiple measures in one tool, which can facilitate broader adoption and reproducibility in cognitive science and psychology. It is particularly valuable for researchers studying decision-making and confidence, as it simplifies complex analyses and encourages standardization. The 17 measures include three meta-d' family estimates (meta-d', M-ratio, M-difference), four nonparametric Type-2 measures (AUC2, Gamma, Phi, delta confidence) with eight SDT-normalized forms, and two model-based measures (meta-noise, meta-uncertainty). Currently, it only supports binary (two-alternative) discrimination tasks, and the package is based on the measures evaluated by Rahnev (2025).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Signal detection theory (SDT) is a framework for measuring the ability to differentiate between signal and noise, commonly used in psychology and neuroscience. Metacognition refers to the awareness and monitoring of one's own cognitive processes, often measured via confidence ratings. Meta-d' is a measure of metacognitive sensitivity that indicates how much evidence is available for metacognitive judgments relative to perceptual sensitivity.

## [MBDiff: Multi-view Behavior-aware Diffusion Model for Utility Data Imputation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29177) ⭐️ 6.0/10

MBDiff is a novel multi-view behavior-aware diffusion model for probabilistic imputation of missing utility data, incorporating a multi-view user behavior extraction module and a behavior-aware conditional diffusion model. It achieves significant improvements over state-of-the-art baselines, with 7.04% and 29.1% gains on electricity and water usage datasets for block missingness imputation. This work addresses a practical problem in utility data management, where missing data can affect billing accuracy, demand forecasting, and supply management. By leveraging user behavior information, MBDiff offers a more accurate imputation approach, potentially improving operational efficiency for utility providers and enabling better data-driven decisions. MBDiff consists of two key components: a multi-view User Behavior Extraction module that learns behavior from global, local, and instance-level views, and a behavior-aware conditional diffusion model with a reference selection module and a conditional attentional denoising network. The model was implemented and evaluated in collaboration with a major municipal utility provider in Florida, demonstrating its practical applicability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Utility data, such as electricity, water, and gas consumption, is collected by sensors and embedded devices, but often contains missing values due to device failures or transmission issues. Data imputation is the process of estimating missing values, and probabilistic imputation provides a distribution of possible values rather than a single point estimate. Diffusion models are a class of generative models that learn to reverse a noising process, and they have been applied to various data generation and imputation tasks.

<a id="item-271"></a>
## [New Theory Unifies SOMA and DE Proposal Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29228) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces an operator-selection factorization that separates objective-independent variation from boundary repair and fitness-dependent selection, deriving closed-form expressions for proposal statistics of SOMA and DE under Bernoulli masks. This theoretical framework provides a deeper understanding of how SOMA and DE generate candidate solutions, potentially guiding the design of more effective evolutionary optimizers and bridging the gap between these algorithms. The canonical SOMA proposal is shown to be affine in the search space and exactly linear in an augmented migrant-leader state, enabling interpretation of interpolation, projection, overshooting, and coordinate masking. Experiments on the BBOB benchmark show operator-guided variants improve upon canonical SOMA and compete with established DE methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: SOMA and Differential Evolution are population-based stochastic optimization algorithms used for continuous optimization. They generate new candidate solutions by combining existing population members, but their internal mechanisms are often treated as black boxes. This paper provides a mathematical lens to analyze their proposal generation, which is the step that creates new candidate solutions before selection.

<a id="item-272"></a>
## [InMyStyle: Small LoRA Models Suffice for Per-User Style Rewriting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29238) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces InMyStyle, a privacy-first system that uses small language models with LoRA adapters to rewrite AI-edited text in a user's style without an instruction prompt at inference. It finds that model size has little effect on quality, with scores plateauing at 0.69 across 0.5B to 7B parameter models. This work challenges the assumption that larger models are always better for style adaptation, suggesting that small, efficient models can deliver comparable quality while preserving privacy and reducing computational costs. It could enable more accessible and personalized AI editing tools for individual users. The system uses multiple local helper LLMs to construct paired training examples and fine-tunes LoRA adapters on base models ranging from 0.5B to 7B parameters. It also incorporates length-aware generation budgets and automatic chunking to handle inputs of varying lengths, and a secondary evaluation with 400 ratings from five LLM judges showed a 20% lower perceived AI-ness score compared to helper-AI inputs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that adds small trainable adapters to a pre-trained model, enabling customization for specific tasks or styles with minimal computational overhead. Style rewriting aims to modify text to match an individual's writing style, often used in AI-assisted editing to make outputs feel more personal. InMyStyle is designed for single-user, privacy-first scenarios, running locally to avoid sending user data to external servers.

## [OsteoCAD: Cloud-Edge Framework for Bone Tumor Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.29266) ⭐️ 6.0/10

OsteoCAD is a modular eHealth framework that provides end-to-end deep learning capabilities for bone tumor segmentation, integrating dataset creation, preprocessing, model training, and inference through a user-friendly interface while securely connecting to remote GPU infrastructures to overcome local hardware limitations. The framework was validated in a real-world case study in Mexico, demonstrating its feasibility in resource-limited clinical settings. This framework addresses key barriers to AI adoption in healthcare, such as limited computational resources and lack of specialized expertise, by democratizing access to deep learning tools. It enables healthcare organizations in resource-limited settings to leverage advanced medical imaging analysis, potentially improving diagnostic accuracy and patient outcomes. The framework is validated through a case study on large bone tumor segmentation in Mexico, showing it can enable DL-powered eHealth solutions without requiring advanced technical expertise or complex local configurations. It uses a cloud-edge architecture, securely connecting to remote GPU infrastructures to mitigate local hardware constraints.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Deep learning has significantly advanced medical image analysis, but many healthcare organizations struggle to adopt it due to limited computational resources and specialized expertise. Human-in-the-loop deep learning integrates human intelligence into the model development and inference process, which can improve accuracy and trust. Cloud-edge frameworks combine local edge computing with remote cloud resources, offering a practical solution for resource-constrained environments.

## [SATViz: Real-Time Visualization of Clausal Proofs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2209.05838) ⭐️ 6.0/10

SATViz is a new tool that visualizes CNF formulas using the variable interaction graph and a force-directed layout, and it can animate clausal proofs to highlight variables in a moving window of recently learned clauses. The tool also supports creating new layouts with adjusted edge weights. SATViz provides a novel way to observe the community structure of SAT instances in real time, which has been linked to instance hardness and clause quality heuristics. This can help researchers and practitioners better understand SAT solver behavior and potentially improve solver performance. The tool uses the variable interaction graph (VIG) and a force-directed layout algorithm to visualize CNF formulas. It can animate clause proofs by continuously highlighting variables that appear in a moving window of recently learned clauses, and it can also create new layouts with adjusted edge weights.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: SAT (Boolean satisfiability) is a decision problem that asks whether there exists an assignment to variables that makes a given Boolean formula true. SAT instances can be represented as graphs, such as the variable interaction graph, where nodes are variables and edges indicate co-occurrence in a clause. Community structure in these graphs has been associated with instance hardness and clause quality heuristics. Visualization tools like SATViz help researchers understand these structures and the behavior of SAT solvers.

## [Solution Space Path Planning: Real-Time Human-Centered ATC Algorithm](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.00064) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces Solution Space Path Planning (SSPP), a real-time, human-centered algorithm for en-route air traffic control that integrates three intent-based conflict detection methods (distance-based, time-interval-based, and zone-based) to generate conflict-free paths. The best-performing variant, SSPPV with zone-based detection, computes paths in 3.69 ms on average in the Dutch Delta sector using a 5 nmi grid. This work addresses the gap between algorithmic research and operational adoption in tactical air traffic control by prioritizing interpretability and real-time performance. It demonstrates how path planning can be designed to align with human controller workflows, potentially facilitating integration into future ATC systems and improving safety and efficiency. SSPP is developed using both vertex-based and edge-based search nodes, resulting in two variants: SSPPV and SSPPE. SSPPV is approximately 3.77 times faster than SSPPE while offering competitive effectiveness, making it suitable for time-critical operations and interactive 'what-if' probing. An extension examines the trade-off between delay minimization and separation requirements.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Air traffic control (ATC) involves managing aircraft to maintain safe separation and efficient flow. Traditional conflict detection and resolution methods often rely on algorithms that may not be easily interpretable by human controllers, hindering adoption. Solution-space displays are a tool that visualizes conflict zones and safe alternatives, aiding controllers in decision-making. This paper extends that concept to automated path planning, aiming to combine computational efficiency with human-centered design.

## [Data-Driven Method Reproduces Individual Motor Signatures for Realistic Motion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.15225) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a fully data-driven approach using long short-term memory (LSTM) neural networks to generate one-dimensional motion that captures the unique motor signatures of specific individuals, validated with real human data from spontaneous oscillatory motion. This work addresses a critical gap in creating realistic human motion for virtual avatars and robots, enabling more natural interactions in applications like rehabilitation, sports, and manufacturing. It also provides a new characterization of individual motor signatures based on motion amplitude, complementing existing velocity-based approaches. The model reproduces both the velocity distribution and amplitude envelopes of the trained individual while remaining distinct from others, as supported by thorough statistical analyses. The approach is validated using real human data from participants performing spontaneous oscillatory motion, and the architecture is based on LSTM neural networks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Human motion modeling is essential for creating realistic virtual avatars and robots that interact in group activities. Recent research has shown that each person has a unique velocity signature, indicating individual motor behaviors are both variable and consistent. However, existing models often provide simplified descriptions, hindering the development of effective cognitive architectures. This work introduces motion amplitude as a complementary characterization and uses LSTM networks to generate individual-specific motion.

<a id="item-277"></a>
## [Robust BAM Training via Subspace Rotation and Regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.11902) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper introduces a gradient-free training algorithm, the Bidirectional Subspace Rotation Algorithm (B-SRA), and two regularization strategies (OWM and GPA) to enhance the robustness of Bidirectional Associative Memory (BAM) against noise and adversarial attacks. This work addresses the fragility of BAM trained with Bidirectional Backpropagation, offering a more robust alternative that could improve reliability in applications like pattern recognition and associative memory. It also opens new directions for building resilient neural architectures. The authors identify orthogonal weight matrices (OWM) and gradient-pattern alignment (GPA) as key principles for robustness, and the SAME configuration combining both achieves the strongest resilience. Experiments were conducted with memory capacities of 50, 100, and 200 associative pairs under various attack scenarios.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 3, 04:00

**Background**: Bidirectional Associative Memory (BAM) is a two-layer neural network that stores and retrieves pattern pairs bidirectionally. Traditional training uses Bidirectional Backpropagation (B-BP), which can be sensitive to noise and adversarial perturbations. The Subspace Rotation Algorithm (SRA) is a gradient-free method previously used for training Restricted Hopfield Networks, and this paper adapts it to BAM.
